The text event of the T key that opened it is not typed (it arrives with the key, in the same batch of events). Ctrl+T, Cmd+T, a held T and the screen of a local game do not open it. After Enter the line stays on the status line for five seconds as "You: ..." (the room's own copy of a player's line is not shown to its sender). The lines of others are shown there too ("Ann: hello", five seconds) and logged on stderr (`Room chat: Name: text`; **at most 20 lines at once and 10 a second**, `RoomChatLog`, then one line "(N more lines were not logged)": the log of a flooded room is not a flood); the log is `NetGame::pregame_chat()`.
* **The match's chat log starts with the waiting room's lines** (after the original's own first entry, the News Flash "Game started! Go get that food!"): when the match begins the application gives the HUD every line of `pregame_chat()` (the last 200 that the lobby kept, this player's own among them, in order, under the players' names; the room's own notices are not repeated), so the players who talked before START find their talk in the log of the match, and the match's chat goes on in the same log. A line is logged **under the name that its sender had when it spoke** (`ChatLine::name`, review fix LOW-1: a player who talked and left, and a newcomer who took the seat, used to be one: the old line carried the newcomer's name); only a line with no name takes the seat's name.

### The chat box of the 16:9 setup screen

The setup screen of the 16:9 picture (960 x 540, `setup_layout.hpp`) has a chat column in its Online variant (the host of a room on the local network, the leader of a server's room) and its Guest variant (the other players), and `Application::sync_room_view` feeds it every frame while a room is on the screen (`MapSelectScreen::set_chat_panel`, `set_fill_footer`; `Application::room_chat_box()` says whether the screen has the box: a wide canvas, a room, an Online or a Guest layout). The classic 640 x 480 setup screen has no box and keeps the status-line display above, unchanged.

* **lines**: the newest 16 lines of `NetGame::pregame_chat()` (the room keeps 200; the box shows what fits, wrapped to its width, newest at the bottom), each "Name: text" (a seat with no name yet: "Seat N") and a room's own notice (`ChatLine::notice()`) as its text alone, styled by the screen as a notice (the status text's size, 14 px, instead of the players' names' 18 px). A player's own line comes back from the room with its own name and appears in the box like everybody else's.
* **typed / caret**: `Application::room_chat()`: the typed text and the caret (a blink every `RoomChatInput::kCaretHalfPeriodMs`, on the application's network clock) are the input box's text while the input is open; closed, the input box is empty.
* **the status line keeps the prompt** while the box is shown: no "Say: ..." typed line, no "You: ..." echo of the line just said, and `NetGame::set_chat_status_mirror(false)` (default on; the application turns it off whenever the box is on screen) stops the copy of the room's lines on the status line for five seconds that the classic page shows. The other notices of the status line (a cancelled start, "Fog of War is on, so START seats no bots.") are not chat and stay there.
* **click zones**: a click in the box's **input box** or its **lines box** (`SetupLayout::chat.input_box`, `.lines`) opens the input (T opens it as before); while the input is open **any left press closes it**, sends nothing and is not a click on the screen, exactly as on the classic page (a touch screen has no Esc). The status box of the wide screen is not a chat zone.
* **the focus rule is the same code** on both pages: while the input is open S, Q, X, Enter and the arrows do nothing and are not typed; Enter sends and closes, Esc closes; for 400 ms after it closes Enter and S start nothing; a held START key never starts a room's match.
* **the fill footer** (Online variant only): for the leader (or a LAN host) with a fill level the foot of the Players' Status box says "Empty seats at START:" and "Easy / Medium / Hard bots" (`Application::fill_bots()`, `net::fill_level_title()`); nothing for a guest whatever its own `--fill-bots` says, nothing without a fill level, nothing in a room with Fog of War (the room seats no bots there, and the status line says so). It follows `Application::set_fill_bots` at once.
* **when the session ends** (Leave, a lost room, the menu) the panel, the typed line and the footer are cleared with the room view; the next room starts with an empty box.


### The view's zoom and the network (v0.1.0)

The mouse-wheel zoom (`VIEW_AND_HUD.md`, "Mouse-wheel zoom"; `view_zoom.hpp`, `Application::set_zoom`) is the player's own view and nothing else: the simulation, the lock-step runner, the bots and every state hash never see it (a network match in which the two machines have different zooms stays bit-identical: `test_zoom_view` "net"). Where it meets the rooms:

* **A match of the network has the zoom levels of a local game** on the same map and screen (`Application::zoom_limits()` has no limit of the kind of match; up to v0.2.0 a network match offered no zoom-out): a server's room, a LAN game, host or guest, and a match that the leader's START filled with bots alike. The levels are the series 2^(k/4) from 2 down to the map's limit, `max(view_w / map_w, view_h / map_h)` (the map's width or height just fills the view); a match starts at the level nearest to the remembered one (`--zoom 0.5` included; the remembered level is kept) and the wheel moves one level a notch. N5.69 (a server's room with the fill), N5.71 (a LAN host's fill) and N5.70 pin it. **The limit depends on the picture's shape**, so in one match a player in the original's 4:3 picture can zoom out to the whole of TREASURE (limit 0.230, 1920 x 1911 of 1920 x 1920 world pixels) while a player in the 16:9 picture sees at most two thirds of it (limit 0.397, 1920 x 1260; at the zoom 1 the 16:9 picture already shows about twice the area): every player can choose either shape (`--aspect`, the settings key, the page's selector), and a player's levels are the player's own.
* **Why nothing limits it**: the zoom is not on the wire and in no hash. **With Fog of War on, every level draws exactly what the zoom 1 draws**: the one world pass (`Renderer::render_world`) serves every level with the same culling and the same gates (a tile that is not explored is dithered, and the enemy ants, their burn overlays, the food, bombs, fire walls, lunchboxes and power-ups, the falling power-ups and the effects on it are not drawn), and the minimap respects the fog too (enemy ants only on explored cells; food, power-ups and fire walls only where explored); `test_zoom_view` "fog" pins it at every level of GAUNTLET and TINY in both pictures. **What the zoom-out adds is breadth, not a new kind of information**: the types and states of the ants, planted bombs, effects and the hit point digits of every ant that is visible, over a wider area at once (positions were already on the minimap). The game still holds the camera inside the levels at every frame, not only at the wheel, the API and the match start: `Application::enforce_zoom_limits()` takes a camera that is outside the map's limit (a new picture moves it) back to the nearest allowed level, anchored at the view's centre, the remembered level untouched; `test_zoom_view` "net".
* **The setup screens take no wheel and no middle button**, the 16:9 screens of a room with their chat box included, over the box, the preview or anywhere else, with the chat input closed or open: nothing zooms, nothing opens or closes the input, the typed line stays (N5.68; the window's event loop does not route a wheel to a setup screen at all, `view_zoom_allowed` is false outside a running match). The camera of a setup screen is at the remembered level from the start (`--zoom 2 --join ...`), so **the map preview of a room's screen is drawn at the zoom 1 whatever the camera is at** (`Renderer::render_world_image` sets the zoom for the picture and restores it; N5.72 for the leader's and the guest's screens).
* **Typing in the match's chat goes on through the zoom**: the characters of a line (`+`, `-`, `=`) are text and never a zoom key; the wheel and the middle button change the zoom in the middle of a line and leave it as typed, and Enter sends it whole to the room (N5.70).
* **The web page** (`web/shell.html`): the wheel, a ctrl + wheel and Safari's pinch are cancelled over the canvas only, and so are the press (`mousedown`) and the click (`auxclick`) of the MIDDLE button (SDL cancels the `mouseup` only; an unprevented middle press over a page that scrolls starts the browser's autoscroll on Windows in Chrome, Edge and Firefox and would defeat "back to zoom 1"; the rest of the page keeps its middle button), on the page with `?join=...&fill=...` as on every other (`tests/scripts/web_fill_check.js` checks that every listener is on the canvas and that `web/lobby.html`, whose frames are `embed=1` pages, has none of its own; `web_aspect_check.py --wheel` checks it in a real browser, with a join address). The chat input of the setup screen is drawn by the game on the canvas, so the page adds nothing for it.

### Measured: the server's CPU with bots

Twelve rooms of one person and three bots each, one simulated client per room in the same process, **only the server's own pass (`RoomManager::update`) timed** over 30 s of play after every room had started (`test_server` S3.71, Release build, Apple silicon, the medians of three runs; the machine was shared with other builds, load average 20 - 30, and the pass was timed with the wall clock, so every figure is an upper bound; the test has since timed the thread's CPU time instead (`CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID`; on Windows the thread's cycle counter, `QueryThreadCycleTime`, which a busy machine does not inflate and which, unlike `GetThreadTimes` (ticks of 15.6 ms, and on some hosted runners lumps of seconds), counts what the thread ran):

| twelve rooms, one person and three bots each | the server's thread per second of play, all twelve rooms (per room) | worst single pass | worst pass while the twelve rooms started |
|---|---|---|---|
| TINY, the leader's fill, medium | 2.95 ms (0.25) | 2.3 ms | 6.9 ms |
| TINY, idle bots (the controller alone) | 3.21 ms (0.27) | 3.8 ms | 1.5 ms |
| TINY, medium bots | 3.28 ms (0.27) | 4.5 ms | 3.1 ms |
| TINY, hard bots | 3.53 ms (0.29) | 2.9 ms | 1.5 ms |
| TREASURE, idle bots | 15.0 ms (1.25) | 7.3 ms | 3.4 ms |
| TREASURE, hard bots | 16.3 ms (1.36) | 6.7 ms | 3.8 ms |

Twelve rooms with 36 bots cost 3 - 16 ms of the server's thread per second of play, 0.3 - 1.6 percent of one core, and most of that is the twelve referee simulations: the bots add about 0.1 ms per second per room on TREASURE with hard bots (the largest of the six maps) and nothing measurable on TINY. No pass came near a turn (50 ms); the start of twelve rooms in one pass, in which each room analyses its map once (`MapInfo`), is a hitch of a few milliseconds. The test bounds only the order of magnitude (under 100 ms a second, no pass of 250 ms) and, in a build with AddressSanitizer or UBSan, which runs the server 10 to 30 times slower, plays two TINY rows and bounds nothing.

### Limits and what is not here

* The fill seats the **standard** bot of one level in every empty seat (since protocol 13 the leader chooses a level for each seat: "Protocol 13", below); a room's specification names its bots seat by seat (`bots`). The standard bot of a level gathers food, raids and fights back, and a Hard one attacks (`docs/BOTS.md`, "The standard bot"); the fill draws every bot's style from its own seat's generator.
* A bot seat is not a person's: it cannot chat, vote or be kicked, the room's `leader` is never a bot, and **a bot cannot be used to hold a room open** (a room whose people have all left ends "everybody left", bots or not).
* The chat has no moderation beyond the chat budget (a burst of 5 lines, then one a second) and the violations, no history for a late joiner, no private lines and no team before the match: it is the original's chat entry (100 printable characters). In the match a line for the team goes to the sender and its ally only (above).
* The classic 640 x 480 setup screen has no chat box (its display is the status line and the typing line, above; the 16:9 screen has the box); the Host panel and the web card choose a level for each seat since protocol 13.
* A server's `max_rooms` and connection limits are as before; a room with bots holds one `BotController` (the analysis of its map, a few kilobytes for the shipped maps) until it ends.

### Tests

`test_lockstep` **N2.89** (the StartRequest of two bytes: every level, every truncation and trailing byte, every level above 3, the names and the parser, the room's notice as Chat from 255, 300,000 mutated StartRequests and chat lines only give what encodes back), N2.1 / N2.3 / N2.3b / N2.40 / S2.5 (rewritten: the version number and protocol 7's one-byte request); `test_lobby` **N4.17** (the fill at the lobby: only the leader's, with one person, ignored by everybody else, garbage layouts, the names) and **N4.18** (the chat of the room: relay, seat of the connection, no team, late joiner, loading, violations, flood, the log, the notice, a LAN host's own line), N4.1 / N4.11 / N4.12 (rewritten); `test_server` **S3.60 - S3.71** (S3.60 one person fills three seats, names, status, the whole match played, the referee's hash equal to the client's at the same tick; S3.61 the rules of who fills when; S3.62 fog; S3.63 a cancelled start; S3.64 a map the filled roster cannot play; S3.65 the control interface's `bots`, every mistake a 400, the JSON; S3.66 a room's own bots and the early start; S3.67 bot seats never absent and never voters, in a room that holds seats; S3.68 chat in the waiting room over the server; S3.69 the door of protocol 11; S3.70 over real sockets: a fill and chat; S3.71 the CPU measurement); `test_netgame` **N3.18** (chat on a room of the local network, the status line, loading, the log kept) and **N3.19** (`NetGame::fill_bots`); `test_ai` **AI5.7** (a LAN host's fill with the real bots over real sockets); `test_network_app` **N5.45 - N5.48** (the command line, the leader with `--fill-bots` and `--say`, one person alone, fog, a LAN host's START with a guest that never answers a ping, a cancelled one and the bots that run on the host's machine) and **N5.49 - N5.52** (the chat input: T and the click open it, the T is not typed, Enter sends and closes, Esc closes, S / Q / X / Enter / the arrows do nothing while it is open and work after, the status line shows the line, a local game has no chat, through the window's own event loop too; the match's chat log starts with the waiting room's lines; the status line's prompt for a START with bots, in a server's room, with fog and on a LAN host); `test_start_menu` **M4.3** and `test_start_menu_app` **A14.1 - A14.2** (the Host panel's choice: cycling, the request, the settings, the room's panel, the geometry; one person alone hosts with Medium bots through the menu and plays; Join fills nothing; `--start-menu --fill-bots`) and the two new panels' fingerprints in `test_view_fingerprint`; and the team-chat tests above; `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` (a real server and real headless clients: a leader alone with `--fill-bots medium --start-when 1` starts three bots at 20 ticks a second, the status lists them, two clients chat in the waiting room with `--say`; the pages' validation of `?fill=` run with node, `tests/scripts/web_fill_check.js`). **The review fixes** (the chat budget, the guard after the input closes, a click closes it, the prompt label's two lines, the seeded names, the log's budget, the caption, the vote, the door's protocol check, no bot controller without bots): `test_lockstep` **N2.93**, `test_lobby` **N4.20**, `test_netgame` **N3.20**, `test_server` **S3.73**, `test_network_app` **N5.53 - N5.59**, `test_start_menu` M4.3. The mutations that were tried are listed in the CHANGELOG (53 of the fill and the room's chat, 38 of the team chat and the screens, 8 of the checks of the web pages, 53 of the review fixes; all caught). **Where the rooms meet the zoom (v0.1.0)**: `test_network_app` **N5.68** (the wheel and the middle button do nothing on the 16:9 screens of a room, leader and guest, over the chat box, its input box, the preview and elsewhere, with the chat input closed and open), **N5.69** (a server's room with the leader's fill: the match starts at 1, no zoom-out, the level stays remembered, the local game after it starts at it), **N5.70** (typing in the match's chat through the wheel, the middle button and the characters `+ - =`), **N5.71** (the same for a LAN host whose bots run on this machine), **N5.72** (the map preview of a room's screens is the zoom 1 picture with the camera at 0.5 or 2), `tests/scripts/web_fill_check.js` and `web_aspect_check.py --wheel` (one page with `?fill=` and the wheel).

## Protocol 12: the match clock waits for the "Get ready to play!" dialog (v0.2.0)

Request of the owner: "I believe on the original game when the get ready box appears the timer is already running, but can we make a change for this remake that the timer doesn't start until the ants actually are able to move? It wastes like five or six seconds." The original runs the match clock and the ants behind the dialog (`docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` section 21: the GO handler that releases the dialog also starts the clock), so a person loses the 5 s of its task KWFO; the remake **deviates on purpose** (`docs/AUDIT_ONE_TO_ONE.md` section 3b): every match still opens with the dialog (its picture, its texts, 5 s, every click and key taken), but **the simulation does not run while it is up**: tick 0 runs when it closes and the clock shows the match's full time.

### The change in the network, and why the number moves

* **No message changed.** `kProtocolVersion` goes from 11 to **12** because the *meaning of the start* did: the host seals the **first turn 5000 ms after the match began** (`net::kMatchStartDelayMs`, which is `sim::kMatchStartDialogMs`), and every machine closes its dialog when its **first turn executes**. A client of 11 counted its dialog in simulation ticks (100 ticks of `HUD::update` from the tick hook) and would have been blocked for 100 ticks of the running match after the host's late first turn, while a bot of the room acts: so a game of 11 must not play with one of 12. It is refused with the existing refusal (`RejectReason::VersionMismatch`, answered by the host's lobby and by the server's door; the LAN list shows a room of another number as another version): a Hello of 11 is byte for byte a Hello of 12, and the number alone refuses it (tests `test_lobby` N4.2c, `test_server` S3.3 and S3.73, `test_netgame` N3.22).
* **The delay is a constant of the protocol, not a field of a message.** No machine needs the number: a client opens its dialog on an event (the match began) and closes it on an event (its first turn executed), so a host that chose another delay would break no client; the number is the host's policy. A field (in `Begin`, which is the type byte and nothing else, or in `Start`) would be one more thing for a decoder to bound and refuse, and both ends agree by construction anyway (the same protocol number is the same constant). `HostSession::Config::start_delay_ms` (default 0, which seals turn 0 at once, as the test rigs want) is what `HostSession::start` adds to the first seal time; the two product paths set `kMatchStartDelayMs`: a LAN host (`NetGame::begin_match`) and a server's room (`Room::begin_match`).

### Who opens and closes the dialog, and when tick 0 runs

| | opens | closes | the simulation starts |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A local game** (single player, `--bot`, the start menu's Single player, the web single game) | `Application::enter_match` (the HUD's dialog, timed) | the HUD, after 100 steps of 50 ms of the frame clock (`Application::update_simulation` steps it in real time) | on the step after the dialog's last one |
| **A room on the local network** (the host and every guest) | `Application::net_begin_match` when the match begins on that machine (the HUD's dialog, until dismissed) | `Application::post_tick`: the machine's own first executed turn | the host seals turn 0 at begin + 5000 ms and turn 1 50 ms later; the host's runner starts with two turns in hand (begin + 5050 ms), a guest's one link delay and its buffer later |
| **A room of the dedicated server** | the same, on every client | the same | the referee's own engine and every client as for a LAN host: the room seals turn 0 5000 ms after `Room::begin_match` |

A game that starts straight into its match (`--map`, for tests and screenshots) has no dialog and starts at once, as before. A match is never shorter for the dialog: the clock, the 12 minutes or the map's own time, runs from tick 0.

### The seconds before the first turn are not a stall

* **Nothing exists to wait for.** A client's runner has not started (it begins when its first turns are in: `LockstepRunner::stalled_ms` is 0 before it), the jitter buffer hears nothing (the lateness of the turns is measured against the first turn, so it stays at one turn), the sequencer has no turn that a seat could be behind (`behind_turns` is 0), the lag policy has nothing to announce, a client sends pings and the host answers them (the 10 s of silence that end a host are far away). On the screen: no "Waiting for the other players...", no "Catching up...", no lag line, no notice (`Application::net_overlay_now` says nothing).
* **The idle rule of the lag policy used to count them (found while making this, fixed).** `HostSession::police_laggards` started a seat's idle clock at the start of the match ("its acks have not moved for 30 s while turns wait for it"); with the first turn sealed 5 s late a seat that never acks would have been dropped 5 s early. The clock is now kept at the present while nothing waits for the seat (`behind == 0`), which changes nothing once turns are sealed (a seat that acks has some turns in flight, a seat that is stuck has a growing backlog). Test `test_lockstep` S2.15.
* **A room that holds seats** (reconnect, protocol 10) does not pause for the quiet seconds; if a seat's connection is lost *during* them the match is paused like any other (the schedule slides), and the first turn is sealed at once when it goes on (after the resume countdown): the dialogs have been up for that long anyway. A player who comes back (a Hello with a key) is handed the match by the catch-up and **sees no dialog**: the dialog belongs to the start (the game's own clients skip it since release B: a rejoin is no start).
* **Host migration** (a room on the local network) while the dialogs are up: the guests elect the lowest living seat; it has never seen a turn sealed, so it keeps no schedule and seals from turn 0 at once; the dialogs close sooner than 5 s on those machines (test `test_netgame` N3.23).
* **The server's wall-clock limits.** `run_ms` counts from the moment the match began (the 5 s are in it; a pause for a seat that is away is not), so a room whose limit is 8 s fails 8 s after the match began, not 13 (test S3.75). `wait_ms` and `load_ms` are before the begin and are unchanged.
* **A command that arrives before the first turn is sealed is discarded, without a violation** (`HostSession::handle_message`: while `start_delay_ms` is set and no turn has been sealed). An honest client sends none: its dialog takes every click and key until its own first turn has run. A modified client or a rig may, and the first version of this release let such a command into turn 0 (the sentence that stood here, "at most the link's delay", left out the person's reaction time and was wrong): a raw seat's `GroupMove` 100 ms into the wait ran at tick 1 while an honest client's earliest order was sealed into turn 3 / 6 / 13 on links of 40 / 120 / 300 ms (it has to receive the first turns, run one, and send), so a modified client could script an opening of up to 64 orders per seat ahead of every honest seat (the independent review of v0.2.0 found it with a loopback driver; with the gate the honest seats' turns are the same). **What is left:** after the first seal a modified client's order is sealed as soon as it arrives, so one that is timed to arrive just after the seal is in turn 1 where an honest client's is in turn 3 or later: it is ahead by the link's delay, the buffer and the person's reaction time, which is the advantage that a scripted client has anywhere in a match, and no longer a scripted opening that runs before anybody can act. **Why the first seal and not "only from a seat that has acknowledged turn 0":** an acknowledgement is only a claim (a modified client sends `TurnAck(0)` the moment after the seal, or resends it from before), so the condition would add no protection against the client it is for, and it would tie a command to the order in which two messages of one frame arrive. What would close the rest is a floor per seat (its measured round trip after the first seal, which no honest order can beat): not built. Tests: `test_lockstep` N2.95 (a host with a seat) and S2.16 (a referee, links of 40, 120 and 300 ms): a raw seat's seventy orders at 100 ms and one that arrives 100 ms (or 10 ms) before the seal are in no turn, one that arrives 10 ms after is in turn 1, the honest seats' orders are sealed into the first turn after they arrive, no violation, and with `start_delay_ms` 0 a command is accepted at once; `test_netgame` N3.24 (a LAN host over real sockets: `NetGame::begin_match` sets the delay); `test_server` S3.77 (a server's room: `Room::begin_match` sets it); each fails without the gate (the proofs are in `docs/audit/B3_notes.md`).

### The clients and the bots

* **The client** (`Application`): `net_begin_match` calls `enter_match`, which opens the dialog with `start_match_modal(true)` (it never closes by itself); `update_simulation` steps the HUD in real time while the dialog is up so that the worker's portrait moves; `post_tick`, which every executed turn calls, dismisses the dialog before anything else. A page that is hidden in the browser (background steps) closes it from the wake-up that runs the first turn. The clock behind the dialog shows the match's full time, and a click on an ant selects nothing until the dialog is gone.
* **The bots** act on simulation ticks only (`BotController::on_tick`), so none looks or orders before the first tick; they look on tick 1 + seat and open with one token (`docs/BOTS.md`, "The opening"). On a server the referee that runs them is ahead of a person's screen by the link's delay and the runner's buffer, so a bot's first order can be applied a few ticks before a person's first click can be; the head start that remains is that delay, not seconds.

### Tests

`test_lockstep` **N2.94** (a host with a seat) and **S2.15** (a host without one, a room that holds seats too): the first turn is sealed exactly `start_delay_ms` after `start()` and nothing before it; the seconds before it are no stall, lag, pause or growth of the jitter buffer; the idle rule does not count them; `test_netgame` **N3.22** (a game on the local network over real sockets: no tick for 5 s, nobody told anything, then every machine's first tick; a raw guest of protocol 11 is refused) and **N3.23** (the host leaves while the dialogs are up); `test_server` **S3.76** (a room filled with Hard bots: the first turn exactly 5000 ms after the match began, no bot order in turn 0), **S3.75** (`run_ms` counts the 5 s; the room's bot controller has the product's hold), S3.3 and S3.73 (protocol 11 is refused); `test_network_app` **N5.74** (a guest's dialog is up exactly while no tick has run and ends with its first turn), **N5.75** (the same for the leader of a server's room), N5.39 (a hidden page's dialog ends on time) and N5.38, **AI6.9** (a local game: the dialog for 100 steps, the simulation waits, tick 1 on the next step), **AI6.10** (a host's room); the HUD's own tests 12.118 and 12.118b. Each was shown to fail without the code it tests (the proofs are in `docs/audit/B3_notes.md`, "The match clock waits for the dialog (v0.2.0)"). Every scenario of the suites that waited for play right after the start waits 5 s longer.

## Protocol 13: a bot level for each seat of the leader's fill, and teams chosen before the start

Requests of the owner: "It would be nice if you could set difficulty for each bot individually instead of one difficulty for all the filled bots" and "You should be able to pre-select the teams in the lobby creation." Games on one computer have both (`docs/BOTS.md`, "Teams at the start of a local game"); this does them for the rooms of the game server and for a room on the local network. No rule of the simulation, no golden hash and no lock-step rule changes: the teams are the original's own two commands, applied the same way on every engine before the first tick, and a match without teams is byte for byte what it was.

### The change in the network

* `kProtocolVersion` goes from 12 to **13**; two layouts change, nothing else (the Room message keeps `{Bot, "Bot (Level)", 0}` for a bot's slot). A Hello of 12 is refused with `VersionMismatch`, as every other version is.
* **StartRequest** (type 24, client to server) is `[24][fill0][fill1][fill2][fill3][team_a][team_b]`: **seven bytes**, a level 0 - 3 (none, easy, medium, hard) for each seat 0 - 3 (the level of the leader's own seat is ignored: a person holds it), and the team pair, `kNoTeam` (255) in both bytes for free for all. The decoder is exact: any other length, a level above 3, one team byte 255 and the other not, a team byte above 3 and two equal seats are no StartRequest (a violation like any garbage).
* **Start** (type 13, host to everybody) is the old layout and **`team_a`, `team_b`** at its end: both 255, or a pair that `sim::plan_start_teams` accepts for the start's roster. The decoder refuses everything else, so a machine never starts another match than the room made: a room only sends a Start whose teams it checked.

### One rule, in `ants_sim` (`include/ants_sim/start_teams.hpp`)

The model of part A moved from `ants_app` (the server cannot use that library): `StartTeams {set, a, b}` with `parse_start_teams` / `start_teams_text` (`ffa`, `A+B`), `plan_start_teams(teams, roster)` (the pairs that are made: the pair, and the other two seats too when both play; or `why` when a seat of the pair does not play or the pair would be the whole match, which the original ends as soon as the pair's score is above 0; `short_why` is the same reason in a form that fits the notice's 100 characters), `local_team_choices` (a local game's), `room_team_choices(players)` (**2 players: free for all only; 3: Green + Red, Green + Blue or Red + Blue; 4: Green with Red, Blue or Black**), `start_teams_title` ("Green + Red against Blue + Black") and `apply_start_teams(engine, teams)`: for each pair the first seat invites and the second accepts with `apply_command`, so the engine's own News Flash "... are a team now!" is posted and no invitation stays open. `ants_app/local_teams.hpp` keeps its names as aliases of these; a local game is unchanged (7.6f, 7.6g).

**Every engine that starts from a Start calls `apply_start_teams` right after `init` and the names, before its first tick**: the machines and a machine that comes back from nothing (`Application::net_load_match`), the referee (`Room::begin_match`) and a restored referee (`Room::begin_replay`, from the Start in the restart record). A machine that comes back with its turns keeps its engine. The state hash covers the alliances, so an engine that did not make them would fail the room at the first hash check (turn 19).

### What the server and a LAN host do at the leader's START

* Only the **leader's** request counts, as before (`early_start`; the teams of a request that does not start the match are ignored). `net::plan_fill_seats(plan, room, players)` is the one rule (the server's `Room::update`, a LAN host's `NetGame::start_match` and the leader's screens all call it): for the seats 0 - 3 in order, an empty seat whose level is not none gets the standard bot of that level while fewer than the room's `players` seats are taken; a seat that a person took meanwhile is skipped and its level ignored. Fog of War refuses the fill with `kNoticeFillFog` as in protocol 11, **the teams still count** for the people who play.
* The teams are checked against the **final roster** (the people and the bots just seated). If they can be made they go into the Start. If not, the match **starts without teams** and every person in the room gets a notice once it has started (a `Chat` from sender 255 like the fill's notices): "**No teams: Black does not play in this match.**" (the seat is named by its colour; the command line's own text, `--teams 0+3: seat 3 does not play in this match.`, has the digit) or "**No teams: only two seats play: a team of them would end the match at once.**" (`kNoticeNoTeams` + `short_why`; a LAN host tells its guests and its own status line the same). A room with no teams of its own that fills up and starts by itself has no request: no teams (a room that has them makes them every time: the next section).
* `RoomStatus` has `teams` ("ffa", "0+2"), the room's own `room_teams` ("0+1" or "") and the referee's `allies` by seat for the tests; the control interface does not show them.

### The room's own teams (a word of its code until protocol 15, its create block's since)

A leader's START carries teams only for a room that its leader starts; a room that **fills with people starts by itself**, with nobody to ask (the owner's main case: four friends, two against two). So the **room has teams of its own**, and makes them for **every** start. Protocol 13 put them in the room's code, a word `t01` of `demo-treasure-4p-t01-k7m2xq` (no new message and no new protocol number: the code was in the Hello already); since protocol 15 a code names nothing, and the teams are a field of the create block of the Hello that made the room (`RoomSpec::teams`, "Protocol 15" below), which every client reads in the Room message.

* **The create block's teams** are `team_a`, `team_b`: both 255 for none, else two different seats, the lower first. The pages and the start menu fill them from their teams (the front page: the pair that its Team 1 and Team 2 buttons make, which the lobby holds in the room; the desktop Host panel: a pair of `room_team_choices`), and the pair is judged against the seats that really play at every start, below. A room made another way (the control interface, or an earlier release) has none. Before protocol 15 they were a word of the code, `demo-treasure-4p-t01-k7m2xq`.
* **The server** sets the room's own teams from the create block when a Hello makes a public room (`RoomSpec::teams`), and `Room::update` asks `sim::start_teams_for(room_own, by_leader, asked)` at every start: **the room's own win**, for the automatic start of a full room and for a leader's START; a leader's request counts only in a room that has none and only when it is what starts the match. A room made through the control interface reads no create block (it has its own map and players) and has no teams of its own.
* **One validity rule.** The teams are checked against the final roster (`plan_start_teams`): when the seats that play cannot make them (a seat that does not play, a room of two) the match starts without teams and everybody in the room is told, "No teams: ..."; the room's teams win even then, the leader's request does not take their place.
* **The clients.** `NetGame::room_teams()` is the teams of the Room message (protocol 15; protocol 13 read the code) and `effective_teams()` is the room's or else its own choice; the leader's prompt says "the room's teams: Green + Red against Blue + Black" (the footer "Room teams:"), and its StartRequest carries the effective teams. A LAN host has no code: its own choice, as before.

### Restart records

The record stores `net::encode(start)`, so the Start with its teams and, since protocol 15, the platforms of its seats; the head's flags byte says whether the room was a public one (`public_room`, bit 2). A record that an earlier release wrote has the older Start layout (protocol 12: no team bytes and no platform bytes; 13 and 14: no platform bytes): its head is read with the missing bytes added as "no teams" and "not told" (only when the head names one of those protocols; the old layout under any other protocol stays unreadable), so it reaches the check that refuses records of another protocol: at a restart it is **not restored** (its match ends, its room is a failed room that names both protocols, the file is kept whole for a day in `refused/`, and the log says so; test S3.128). A restored room makes the same teams and the same bots of their own levels.

### The client

* `--fill-bots` takes one word (every seat, as before) or **four words for the seats 0 - 3** (`none,none,easy,hard`, any case); anything else is a startup error. `--teams ffa|A+B` is accepted with `--join`, `--join-url` and `--host` now: it is this machine's choice for a START that it leads (a guest's is ignored by the room). `NetGame::set_fill_bots(FillPlan)` (one level still sets all four) and `set_start_teams`, both sent by `request_start`; a leader with no other person still needs at least one seat filled to start.
* **What a person sees.** The leader's status line says the plan in the longest of five ways that fits its label (293 px, two lines, on the classic page; 363 px on the 16:9 one): "Press START: Blue gets an Easy bot, Black a Hard bot; teams Green + Red against Blue + Black." (one level in every empty seat keeps "Press START: the empty seats get Medium bots."; **teams that the room has of its own are said as the room's**: "...; the room's teams: Green + Red against Blue + Black." and the leader's own `--teams` is shown nowhere in such a room), and the 16:9 waiting room's footer says it in two lines in the largest of three fonts that fits the box ("Empty seats at START:" / "Medium bots"; "Bots: Blue Easy, Black Hard" / "Teams: Green + Red against Blue + Black"; the room's: "Room teams:" / "Green + Red against Blue + Black" and "Room teams: Green + Red against Blue + Black" in the second line). At the start the engine's News Flash tells each team; no dialog opens.
* **The desktop Host panel** has a row for each seat after the leader's (**Red / Blue / Black at START**: Leave empty, Easy bot, Medium bot, Hard bot) and, for three or four players, a **Teams** row with the room's choices; the settings file holds `host_fill` (one word: every seat after the leader's, as the file of an earlier version has it; or four words) and `host_teams` (`ffa` or `A+B`). The room's panel says "Empty seats will be Medium bots." (one level in every seat), "At START: Red gets an Easy bot, Black a Hard bot." and "Room teams: Green + Red against Blue + Black." (the Teams row's choice is in the room's create block: the room makes it for every start, not only at START).
* **The web front page's earlier card** (until v0.12.0; the lobby that replaced it keeps the plan and the teams in the room, "Protocol 16", and its links carry the code only; what follows is what the earlier card did; the old test room is gone) had, for each seat that is not You, a group of five buttons (Friend, Easy, Medium, Hard, Nobody: "The front page", above) and, for three or four playing seats, a Team 1 and a Team 2 switch (two seats on the same switch are a team; with four seats the other two are the other team); they are remembered as `ants-match` (JSON: `sides` holds a number for each seat, 0 for neither switch, 1 for Team 1, 2 for Team 2, next to the `teams` word that they make; an older saved choice that has only `teams` shows that pair on Team 1). Every link of a card's room, the invitations and START, carries `&fill=<plan>` (four words for the seats 0 - 3: a bot's level, `none` for You, a Friend and Nobody; nothing when no seat has a bot) and `&start=<people>`; **the teams are in the create block that every link of the card's room carries** (`&roomteams=0%2B1`, "Protocol 15" below), so no link carries `&teams=`, and `web/shell.html` hands `&fill=`, `&start=` and, for a room whose block names no teams, `&teams=A%2BB` to the game as `--fill-bots`, `--start-when` and `--teams` through whitelists (`antsFillPlanArg`: one level word or four words; `antsStartArg`: one digit 1 - 4; `antsTeamsArg`: two different seats 0 - 3). An old `?fill=medium` link works as before. **An address `?room=<code>&teams=A+B`**: a room whose block names teams uses its own and `&teams=` is ignored; when the block gives the seats the pair is narrowed to them (a pair that the room cannot offer is dropped); for a code with no block the page cannot know the room and passes it on as it is (the game and the server judge). The room panel says "Teams of this room: Green + Red against Blue + Black. They are part of the room (set when it was made), so the match starts with them every time: when the room fills up as well as at START." for the block's teams and "Teams at START: ..." for an address's; "Every link of this room carries the bots; only the leader's START uses them." for the plan.

### Limits

* A room's teams are chosen **before** the start, one pair as in a local game (the two other seats too when both play); players still team up in the match with the team-up button, as before. They are in the room's create block, so a room made by a page or by the start menu makes them for every start; a room made any other way (a room of the control interface, or a room of an earlier release) has the leader's START choice when the leader starts it early, and none when it fills up and starts by itself.
* **The plan names four seats; the desktop Host panel's rows are Red, Blue and Black** (it names the seats after the leader's own, seat 0, which is Green), the front page names all four. A leader who sits elsewhere through a seat link (`&seat=`) gets the plan's seats as they are named: the level of the seat that the leader holds is ignored (a person holds it) and every other empty seat that has a level gets its bot, a bot at Green too (test N5.84). Nothing is seated where a person is, and the levels never move to another colour.
* A client does not know a server room's player count (the Welcome says four seats), so the leader's line names the seats that the plan would fill if every one were free; the server's cap (the room's `players`) decides, and the room's panel on the desktop uses the room's own count.
* A match that was running under a server of protocol 12 does not survive the update to 13: its record is refused for its protocol (above), so the match ends and its players make a new room.

### Tests

`test_commands` **N1.23** (the model: parse, plan, choices, titles), **N1.24** (`apply_start_teams` is the hand-made commands, the News Flash of each pair, no invitation stays open, nothing for teams that cannot be made, the hash covers the teams) and **N1.25** (`start_teams_for`: the room's own win, a request counts only when it starts the match and only in a room without teams, over every combination); `test_lockstep` **N2.100** (both messages, every byte, every team, every roster) with N2.3 / N2.3b / N2.89 (the canonical-encoding fuzzes), `test_lobby` **N4.17b** / **N4.17c** (the leader's plan reaches the owner, the lobby's request, the plan's text and rule) and N4.1 / N4.2b / N4.17; `test_netgame` **N3.34** / **N3.35** (a LAN host's bots and teams, the prompt and footer texts) and **N3.36** (a LAN host, and a client that has been told no room yet: the machine's own choice of teams counts; the room's words in the prompt and the footer; the Room message's teams are RJ1.26's); `test_server` **S3.125** (a level for each seat: the names, the lowest seat first, a taken seat skipped, the cap, fog), **S3.126** (the teams on every engine before the first tick, the machines' hashes equal the referee's to the end, impossible teams and their notice), **S3.127** (a restart record keeps both), **S3.128** (a record of 12 is refused for its protocol and kept, any other old layout is unreadable) and **S3.129** (the room's own teams: a room that fills with four people starts by itself with them on every engine and the referee, an early START the same, precedence over a leader's request, a pair that the seats cannot make, what a block may name edge by edge); `test_rejoin` **RJ1.3b** (a machine that rejoins from nothing has the teams); `test_network_app` **N5.77** (`--fill-bots`), **N5.78** (a server room with two games, two bots of their own levels and teams: every engine has the alliances at tick 1, the News Flash once per machine, equal hashes), **N5.79** (the LAN host the same) and **N5.80** (the texts fit with the real font, for a START's teams and for the room's) **N5.81** (a leader who joins a room whose block names teams, two games and a bare machine that fill one up and start it by itself) and **N5.82** (the 16:9 footer, and a room with no teams of its own gets the leader's START choice); `test_start_menu` **M4.3** / **M4.5** / **M4.6** (the Teams row's choice goes into the create block), `test_start_menu_app` **A14.1 - A14.4** (through the menu to a real server), `test_wide_pages` (the Host panel's texts with the real font) and the web checks (`web_lobby_check.js`, `web_fill_check.js`, `web_name_check.js`, `test_web_lobby.py`, and a real-server run in `test_ants_server.sh`); `tests/scripts/test_source_portability.py` keeps GCC and Clang builtins out of the C++ sources (MSVC has none).

## Protocol 14: the leader of a room moves a player to another colour

Request of the owner (2026-10-05): "how can the host change peoples color forcefully". A colour is a seat, and a player's seat was the one it asked for in its Hello (the page's `?seat=`) or the first free one: nothing moved a player afterwards. The smallest thing that works with a finger: the leader presses a player's row of its setup screen, and that player goes to the next colour that nobody holds. No rule of the simulation changes (a match is made from the seats as they are at START).

### The change in the network

* `kProtocolVersion` goes from 13 to **14**; one message is new, nothing else changes (protocol 15 followed with the short room codes and changed this message: "Protocol 15" below). A Hello of 13 is refused with `VersionMismatch`, as every other version is.
* **SeatMove** (type 31, client to server) is `[31][from][to]`: **three bytes**, two different seats 0 - 3, "put the player that holds `from` in the empty seat `to`" (protocol 15 added a four-byte guard and lets `to` hold a guest: "Protocol 15" below). `MsgType::Last` is 31 now. The decoder is exact: any other length, a seat above 3 and two equal seats are no SeatMove (a violation like any garbage).
* The answer is the **Room message** that every guest gets anyway (each with its own `you` and `leader`); the leader is sent nothing else. The player who was moved is told by the room, a notice in the waiting room's chat (`Chat` from sender 255): "Ann moved you to Red." The leader moving itself is told nothing.

### What the room does (`HostLobby::move_seat`)

* Only the **leader of an open room** is heard: the room waits, it is not loading or running. The whole guest moves to the empty seat with everything that is its own: its connection, **key**, name, thumb, place in the order of the Welcomes (so the leader stays the leader), violations and budgets. A Hello with a key takes the guest's seat as it is now, whatever `want_seat` says (test RJ1.25). A START that was heard earlier in the same pass was made for the room as it was: its plan of bots goes with the colours (the levels of the two seats trade places) and its sender, the leader, is where it sits now.
* **In protocol 14 a colour that anybody holds is never taken** (a guest, a bot, the host): nobody is displaced, and two players change places through the free colours (three presses in a room of three people, four in a room of two); protocol 15 lets two guests change places at once (a bot's colour and the host's are still never taken). A move that cannot be done (the sender does not lead, the room is loading, the seat holds no person, the target is held) is ignored and counted in `ignored_seat_moves`: the first 16 of a connection cost nothing, each one after that is a violation, and none of them spends the budget of the moves that are done. A move that is done is shown to the whole room, so it has a budget like a person's chat (`kSeatMoveBurst` 6, then `kSeatMovesPerSecond` 4; the excess is dropped and counted by nobody, a connection that goes on beyond `kSeatMoveExcessBurst` is a violation). In a running match a SeatMove is a late message: ignored and counted, the same ladder (`HostSession`).
* `RoomStatus`, and so the control interface, has `seat_moves` (the colours that the room moved) and `ignored_seat_moves` ([`SERVER.md`](SERVER.md)).

### The client

* **The leader's screen** (the classic page and the 16:9 one): a press on a player's row of the Players' Status box, and a release on the same row, calls `NetGame::request_move_seat(seat)`. The row lights under the pointer and the press plays the buttons' click. The player goes to the next free colour (green, red, blue, black, around); when every colour is taken the player changes places with the next guest (protocol 15; in protocol 14 nothing happened). The leader's own row works too. A press holds the seat that it was made on: when the rows close up before the release (a player left), the release moves nobody. A guest's screen, a screen that has not shown its players yet (500 ms), one that START has locked, and the row of nobody do nothing.
* **One request at a time**: a press within half a second of the last request is held back (a double click is one press: its second would move the player that the rows close up to, or the same player twice), and the next one waits until the room shows the change or a second has passed (a request that the room cannot do is not answered at all). START waits for the same answer: a START that is pressed meanwhile gets the can't-go cue, and the next one carries the plan as the answer made it.
* **The plan's bots follow what the room shows**: the plan of the leader's fill (`FillPlan`, protocol 13) is by colour, so when the room shows a person in a free colour (a person's colour is empty now, an empty colour is a person's, nothing else changed: `NetGame::follow_moved_player`) the levels of the two seats trade places, and the match that START makes has the same people and the same bots as the leader saw (the Hard bot that was for Blue is for Red when the friend takes Blue). The plan follows the room, not the request: a request that the room did not do changes nothing, and one that was sent twice moves the plan once. Each Room message is compared with the one before it, however many arrive in one update, so a person who comes or goes in another message is no move and a move is not lost among them. A START waits for the answer of a request that is out (above); one that went out first and crosses a request on the wire (a row pressed after START, before the screen locks) is put right by the room (see above).
* **A hint**: when the second person is in the room the leader's status line says "Tap a player to change their colour." for five seconds.

### Limits

* A LAN or direct host cannot move its guests (it holds a seat itself and has no leader), and only the leader moves players: no other player, no vote. There is no colour picker and no kick (a swap since protocol 15).
* A SeatMove of protocol 14 names a seat, not a person: when the player that the leader meant had left and a newcomer took its seat before the press arrived, the newcomer was the one that moved (a colour that is held is never taken, so nobody was pushed out of theirs). Protocol 15's guard closes this: the room acts only on the seating that the leader saw.
* Teams belong to colours (the room's own teams, `--teams`, `&teams=`): a move across the team line changes the player's side, and the bot that the plan keeps goes to the side that the player left. The player's notice names the colour only.
* In the flow of the earlier card (and of the old test room, now gone) the leader's game presses START by itself when the last friend arrives (`&start=`), so there is no time to move anybody; a room that the leader starts by hand has all the time.
* A game of a window or a tab of its own puts the seat that the room gave it in its address, as every game page does, so a reload takes that seat and its key. A game in a frame of `web/lobby.html` cannot (the page that holds the frame makes its address): the page learns the seat from the game's sync reports, which a running match sends, and "Play here" again reconnects as that seat; a frame that is moved in the waiting room and reconnects before the match starts asks for its first seat again, and takes it back if it is free.
* The match's chat log keeps the colour that a line had when it was said. In a room whose cap is below four players the order in which START seats the plan's bots can drop another bot than before the move.

### Tests

`test_lockstep` **N2.101** (the layout, the guard and every pair of seats: exactly the 12 pairs of two different seats are accepted, a guard of 0 is not), N2.1 / N2.3 / N2.40 / N2.100 (the version, the fuzz, `peek_type`), **S2.5b** (a SeatMove in a running match) and S2.6 (the flood ladder); `test_lobby` **N4.22** (the move with key, thumb and notices; what cannot be done refused (N4.23 has the swap); the leader moves itself; a START in the same pass and the plan of bots that it carries; a key after a move; the ladder, and the budget that only the moves that are done spend; a nameless leader; a LAN host; garbage); `test_netgame` **N3.38** (the target colour for every room) and **N3.39** (the plan follows the room message by message, with a server that is a script: a move trades the levels, anything else trades nothing, messages read together are compared one by one); `test_server` **S3.26b** (a room of three: the moves, START, each machine plays its colour, the hashes agree) and S3.30 (the status's counters); `test_rejoin` **RJ1.25** (a moved guest rejoins at its new seat); `test_network_app` **N5.91** (the press on both pages, the plan, the hint, a guest's screen), **N5.92** (a double click is one press: the half second, the room's answer or a second; the plan follows the room once however many requests went out, and not at all for a request that the room ignored), **N5.93** (a press holds the seat of the row's player: the rows close up and the release moves nobody), **N5.94** (the hint gives way to the loading screen; a net game that joins another room starts afresh), **N5.96** (START waits for the answer of a colour change, a second at most; with the plan as the answer made it) and **N5.97** (the hint gives way to a line that is showing; START takes away the hint and nothing else); `web_name_check.js` (a frame of the room panel reconnects as the seat that its game reports).

## Protocol 15: one link makes a room, short room codes and the leader's swap (v0.11.0)

Requests of the owner (2026-10-05, and 2026-10-07 for the length): the join code ("the 8 digit code join", which became six letters and numbers in two groups of three) and "everybody enters on the same link, and then the host is able to rearrange the slots". What a room was lived in its code (`demo-treasure-4p-t01-k7m2xq`: the map, the players and the teams were words of it, 27 characters that nobody can say aloud), and a leader could only move a player to a free colour. No rule of the simulation, no golden hash and no lock-step rule changes: a match is made from the seats as they are at START.

### The change in the network

* `kProtocolVersion` goes from 14 to **15**; four layouts change. A Hello of 14 is refused with `VersionMismatch`, as every other version is (the door reads a Hello's version and name first and nothing else of it).
* **Hello** is the layout of protocol 14, then `platform` (one byte) and, for a join that has chosen one, the **create block** `[map_name str8][seats][team_a][team_b][flags]`. The block ends the message and is read strictly: a map name that is empty or valid, seats 2 - 4, both team bytes 255 or two seats `a < b < 4`, no flag but bit 0 (`kCreateLeaderStarts`); anything else, and every truncation, is no Hello (a violation like any garbage). The lobby writes the block in every Hello of a join that set one, a Hello that shows a key included; the server ignores it there (a Hello with a key never makes a room), and the Hello of a reconnect (`NetGame::way_back_hello`) carries none.
* **Room** (server to guests): every seat ends with a **platform byte**, and the message ends `... you, leader, team_a, team_b, flags`: the room's own teams (both 255: none) and its rule bits (`kRoomLeaderStarts`, bit 0). Every screen shows what the room is, and no client reads a code for it.
* **Start** ends with the four platform bytes of the seats (0 for a seat that does not play).
* **SeatMove** is `[31][from][to][guard]`: **seven bytes**, with a 32-bit **guard**, little endian and never 0 (below).
* **The platform byte** is the operating system in its low four bits (0 not told, 1 Windows, 2 macOS, 3 Linux, 4 Android, 5 iOS, 6 other) and bit 4 for a game that runs in a web browser (`net::platform_text` says it in words). It is told and never checked, and no rule depends on it: a screen may show it as an icon. A seat that holds no person (an empty one, a bot) says 0: the Room decoder refuses anything else for it, and the Start decoder (which does not know who is a bot) takes any valid byte for a seat that plays and refuses one for a seat that does not. A game tells it with `--platform`: a native build tells its own system, a game in a web page says a browser on some system, and `web/shell.html` passes on the `platform` word of the address when it has one (no page of the site writes it yet).

### The room code is only a name, and the create block makes the room

* **The code.** The pages and the desktop menu make **six random characters** of `abcdefghjkmnpqrstuvwxyz23456789` (31 symbols, 8.9e8 codes; no `i`, `l`, `o`, `0` or `1`) and show them in two groups of three, `k7m 2xq` (`room_code_display`); what is typed or pasted loses its inner blanks (`check_room_code`), and its case is never changed. For the server a code is any `valid_room_code` string (1 - 32 letters, digits, `_` and `-`): a code that an earlier release made (`demo-small-2p-x7k2`) is a code like any other and names nothing but itself. A visitor's room takes a code with **no upper-case letter** only (`net::public_room_code`, the one rule of the door and of the clients' texts), which is what the pages and the menu make; the codes that the control interface draws (`new_code`, `draw_room_code`: six symbols of an alphabet without look-alikes, the first always a letter) have a capital, and an operator who names a room by hand gives it one, so that a Hello with a block cannot take the name of a room that is yet to be made and seat its players in the visitor's room.
* **The door** (`RoomManager::route_hello`). A Hello for a code that is no room **makes one** when the server has public rooms with a place free (`--demo-rooms N`), the Hello carries a create block, it shows **no key** and the code has **no upper-case letter**; in every other case the answer is `NoSuchRoom` (a typed code, a Hello with a key, a code with a capital, a server without public rooms, a room of the control interface that is over). The first Hello decides: a room that exists ignores the block of any later Hello, so a link to a room never changes it. A public room that is over is forgotten when a Hello with a block comes to its code, and that Hello makes a new room (a friend who comes late, a reload, a rematch with the same link); a Hello with a key neither makes a room nor replaces one (RJ1.6, RJ1.21).
* **What the block chooses.** The seats are the room's players (2 - 4). The map is the block's when the server lists it (`--demo-maps`, compared in any case) and the server's own (`--demo-map`) when it does not, so a page that offers a map that this server lacks still gets its room, and the first Room message tells which map the room has (the desktop menu compares and says so). The teams are the room's own, as protocol 13 made them, now from the block. With `kCreateLeaderStarts` a room that is **full does not start by itself**: only its leader's START starts it (`RoomSpec::leader_starts`, `HostLobby::Config::leader_starts`, told to every client as `kRoomLeaderStarts`), so that the leader can arrange the colours first; the room's ten minutes to fill still run (a full room whose leader never presses START ends), and a room that allows no early start has no leader and starts by itself as ever. The limit of public rooms, the ten-minute wait, the pause cap and the eviction of an abandoned room are those of the demo rooms ([`SERVER.md`](SERVER.md#demo-rooms)).
* **Restart records.** The head's flags byte has `public_room` (bit 2: the room counts against the cap again after a restart). The Start of an earlier layout is read for the releases that wrote it (12, 13 and 14: "Protocol 13", Restart records), and such a record is refused for its protocol, as any other is.
* **The clients** (`NetGame::set_create`, `set_platform`). `--room-map`, `--room-seats`, `--room-teams`, `--room-leader-start` and `--platform` make the block and the byte of a join ([`COMMAND_LINE.md`](COMMAND_LINE.md)). The web page's links carried the block as `roommap`, `roomseats` and `roomteams` (and `roomleaderstart`, `platform`) while the test room existed; nothing on the front page writes it now, and `web/shell.html` still lets each through its own whitelist ([`PLAY_IN_BROWSER.md`](PLAY_IN_BROWSER.md)). The desktop Host panel sends the block of its choices with the Hello of its new code. A game whose Hello carried a block for a code that a block can make a room of (`public_room_code`) and was answered `NoSuchRoom` says "The server cannot make a room now: it is busy, or hosts no online matches. Try again in a few minutes." (the desktop menu: "The server could not make a room now: it is busy, or it does not host online matches. Try again in a few minutes."); one that carried none, or a block with a code that has a capital (a room of the control interface that is over: a block never makes it), says "There is no such room on this server." (the menu: "There is no room with the code k7m2xq on <server>. Check the code (capital letters matter).")

### The leader's colour moves swap, and carry a guard

* A SeatMove puts the player of `from` in `to`: a free colour takes the player (as in protocol 14), and **a colour that another guest holds is swapped with it**. A bot's colour and the host's are never taken (a public room has no bot before START, a room that the control interface made may seat the bots of its specification from the start, and a server's room has no host).
* **The guard** is `net::seating_hash(room)`: FNV-1a over the state and the name of each seat, with a 0 after each name, and never 0. The leader's screen computes it from the Room message that it shows, the room from its own seats, and the room acts **only when the two are equal**. A press that was made for another seating (the player that it meant has left and a newcomer sits there, a Room message is still on its way) is ignored and counted in `ignored_seat_moves` like any move that cannot be done, so a press moves or swaps only a player that looks to the room as the leader's screen showed it. The guard cannot tell two players apart who have the same name in the same seat, which also look the same on the leader's screen; the pointer screen therefore remembers the name of the pressed row and drops a release over a row whose player has changed meanwhile (`MapSelectScreen::row_pressed_name_`). Round trips, platforms, teams and flags are not in it: they change all the time, or seat nobody.
* **A swap exchanges the whole guests** (`HostLobby::move_seat`): connection, key, name, thumb, platform, place in the order of the Welcomes (the leader stays the leader), violations and budgets. Each guest that was moved, but the leader, is told in the waiting room's chat ("Ann moved you to Red."; in a swap the other one hears "Ann moved you to Green."). A START that was heard earlier in the same pass is made for the room as it was: a move trades the levels of the plan's two colours, a swap trades none (both seats keep a person), and the leader's own seat is where it sits now. The budget is protocol 14's (a burst of 6, then 4 a second).
* **The client.** `NetGame::request_move_seat(from, to)` sends `SeatMove{from, to, seating_hash(room)}` for the leader of an open room, from a guest's colour to a free colour or to another guest's (a move from a colour that holds no guest, a move to a bot's colour and a move to the same colour send nothing; one request at a time, half a second apart, and the next waits for the room's answer for a second at most, as in protocol 14). `request_move_seat(seat)`, the press of a row, goes to `seat_move_target`: the next free colour, else the next guest's colour (the swap), else nowhere.

### Limits

* A code names nothing, so a mistyped code of a room that does not exist is `NoSuchRoom` even on a server with public rooms: only a Hello that carries the block makes a room (the links of the pages, the desktop menu's Host, `--room-map` and its kin).
* Codes with an upper-case letter are never a visitor's: a Hello with a block for one is `NoSuchRoom` (the control interface's codes have capitals). A code that is made without one, whoever makes it first, is a visitor's, so an operator who wants a room of the control interface under a name of their own gives it a capital.
* The block of the first Hello decides: two players who open different links of one code (an address edited by hand) get the room of the first.
* The platform byte is the client's word. A server never checks it and no rule may use it.
* A code is not a secret. There are about 890 million six-character codes (31 symbols, `kRoomCodeChars`), and whoever types the code of a room that waits and has a free seat joins it; the room holds nothing private and its leader sees everybody who joined. A key (which a code is not) is what keeps a seat of a running match.
* A swap is the leader's: there is no vote, no colour picker and no kick, and a LAN or direct host, which has no leader, cannot move anybody.

### Tests

`test_lockstep` **N2.101** (SeatMove: the layout, the guard, every pair of seats, every refusal; the guard is the seating hash, with three answers written down: a change of the hash is a change of the protocol), **N2.102** (the Hello: the platform byte, the create block, the strict decoder, the layout of protocol 14), **N2.103** (the Room and the Start: platforms, the room's teams and flags, every refusal) and **N2.104** (the platform and the block as values: the words of `--platform`, `platform_text`, one spelling for a pair); `test_lobby` **N4.23** (the swap and the guard: both guests with all that is theirs, the notices, a stale press, a bot's colour, a START in the same pass), **N4.24** (the room's rules in the Room message) and **N4.25** (the platforms: from a Hello into the Room message for everybody, a takeover that tells its own or none, the Start, a byte that is no platform); `test_netgame` **N3.17** (the Hello's block and platform, and the text of a refusal that carried a block), **N3.17b** (a host with a seat tells its own platform), **N3.36** (a LAN host's teams, and the leader's prompt and footer; the room's teams from the Room message are RJ1.26's), **N3.38** (the colour that a tap asks for, with the swap) and **N3.40** (`request_move_seat(from, to)` against a scripted server); `test_server` **S3.10**, **S3.23 - S3.25** and **S3.74** (public rooms: only with a block, the limit, the map, the seats, a room that is over, no code with a capital), **S3.24b** (a full room that waits for its leader), **S3.24c** (the platform byte through a real room, a swap and a leave), **S3.26b** (the swap and the guard in a real room: the match runs with the seats as they are), **S3.129** (the room's teams from the block) and **S3.128** (the records of 12, 13 and 14); `test_rejoin` **RJ1.6**, **RJ1.19 - RJ1.21** (a Hello with a key makes no room and replaces none; the place of an abandoned room) and **RJ1.26** (a client of a room that has teams of its own); `test_network_app` **N5.76b**, **N5.81**, **N5.82** and **N5.86** (the options of the create block and of the platform), with the pointer test of `test_wide_setup` (a press whose row has another player when it is let go of moves nobody); `test_start_menu` **M3.3**, **M4.4**, **M4.6** and **M5.2** (the codes and the block) with `test_start_menu_app` **A3.1**, **A4.1**, **A4.2**, **A4.4**, **A4.6** and **A5.4** (through the menu to a real server); the web checks (`web_lobby_check.js`, `web_name_check.js`, `web_fill_check.js`, `test_web_lobby.py` and the browser checks of `tests/scripts/`) and the `options` and `demo` parts of `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh`.

## Protocol 16: the lobby room the front page waits in (v0.12.0)

Requests of the owner (2026-10-07): the front page "should almost be the game lobby": a visitor is put into a room as soon as the page opens, invites people with one link, the people land in a place and the host drags them to the colours that suit, and each player has a Team 1 / Team 2 button; there is no "the room is not open yet" ("this is just the pregame"); and when the host leaves, somebody else is promoted, and a room that nobody is in is closed. Until now a room was made by the first player's Hello at the moment the match was chosen, the waiting room was the game's own setup screen, and nothing showed the leader's choices (bots, teams) to the other players before START. No rule of the simulation, no golden hash and no lock-step rule changes: a match is made from the seats and the plan as they are at START.

### The change in the network

* `kProtocolVersion` goes from 15 to **16**; four layouts change and three messages are new (Plan, Name and Remove). A Hello of 15 is refused with `VersionMismatch`, as every other version is.
* **Hello** is protocol 15's layout with a **client kind** byte between the platform byte and the create block: `kClientGame` (0), a client that can play a match, and `kClientPage` (1), the front page's lobby, which cannot (it waits in a lobby room and sends the leader's choices; its people go to the game page when the leader starts). Anything else is no Hello. The create block has a second flag, **`kCreateLobby`** (bit 1); a block that has it must also have `kCreateLeaderStarts` and 4 seats (`valid_create_block`), and its teams are ignored by the server (the plan decides).
* **Welcome**: the flags byte can carry **`kWelcomeCreated`** (bit 1): this very Hello made the room (a Hello that found the room, even one that was empty, does not say it). It is set for every room that a Hello makes, so a game's Hello that makes a public room (protocol 15) gets it too; a page whose old link got a new room tells it from a join by this bit. It needs a key, as the rejoin bit does, and the two never come together.
* **Room** ends with **four plan bytes** (`PlanKind`: 0 Open, 1 Easy, 2 Medium, 3 Hard, 4 Nobody, what the leader made each colour; for a colour that a person holds it is what comes back when the person goes) and the **`in_game` mask** (bit s: the person of seat s runs a game, not a page; only a seat that a person holds). The room's `flags` have **`kRoomLobby`** (bit 1: the room is a lobby room) and **`kRoomStarting`** (bit 2: the leader asked for START and the room waits for every person's game; only with `kRoomLobby`). The decoder refuses a plan above 4, a game for a seat that holds nobody, `kRoomStarting` without `kRoomLobby` and, in a room that is no lobby room, any plan but Open (0) and any game: a room that is no lobby room behaves as it did in protocol 15, and shows an all-Open plan and an empty mask, which makes its Room message five bytes longer. The room's teams (`team_a`, `team_b`) are the leader's teams in a lobby room and change with the plan.
* **Plan** (type 32, client to server): `[32][map str8][plan0 .. plan3][team_a][team_b]`: the leader's plan, which is the map (a name the server offers; empty, or a name it does not offer, keeps the room's), what each colour is and the teams (255 and 255, or two seats a < b < 4). Nothing else is a Plan: a kind above 4, a map name that is no map name, a missing or extra byte, teams that are not none or a < b < 4.
* **Name** (type 33, client to server): `[33][name str8]`: a person's own new name, 1 - 32 printable ASCII characters (0x20 - 0x7E) with no space at either end. Nothing else is a Name: an empty or a longer name, a character outside the table, a space at an end, a missing or an extra byte. Any person who holds a seat is heard (the leader or not, a page or a game), in an open lobby room whose START does not wait; the room shows the new name to everybody in its next Room message, and says nothing when it is the name the person has already. A name that looks like a bot's (`Bot (`, in any case, the spaces left out) is not taken: the person keeps the name they have, and it is no offence. The name stays with the seat, so a Hello with its key (a game that takes the seat over) does not change it. The names are part of what a colour move's guard is made of (`seating_hash`), so a move that the leader made for the room as it was before a rename is ignored (and counted), and the leader's next one, made for the room as it shows, is done. Budget: a burst of 3, then 1 a second; 6 more are tolerated and each one after that is a violation; a name that cannot be heard (START waits, a match loads or runs) is no offence at first and counts in `ignored_names`, each one after 16 is a violation. In a room that is no lobby room a Name is garbage.
* **Remove** (type 34, client to server): `[34][seat][guard u32]`, six bytes: the leader takes the person of `seat` (0 - 3) out of the room. The guard is a colour move's: the `seating_hash` of the room as the leader's screen shows it, never 0, so the request acts on exactly the person that the leader pressed on (a newcomer who took the colour, a swap or a new name makes it another number, and the request is ignored; like a colour move's guard it cannot tell two persons apart who have the same name in the same colour, so when one of them leaves and another with that name takes the colour, the removal hits the newcomer, who is a new person without a ban). Nothing else is a Remove: a seat above 3, a guard of 0, a missing or an extra byte. Only the leader of an open lobby room is heard, and only for a person who is not the leader (an empty colour and a bot's hold nobody); a person whose seat is held is removed as well, a page as a game is. It is heard **while the leader's START waits for the games**, which is what it is for: a person whose game never opens goes, and the START goes on without them when every person who is left is a game (it ends quietly when the room could not start without them); a match that loads hears none (it is ignored and counted, as below), and one that runs treats a Remove like any late Plan or Name: as a violation of the running session (one of its eight). What it does: the person is sent `Reject` with `Kicked` and their link is closed, their colour is free at once (what the plan calls it stays, and so do the teams), their key is forgotten, the room is shown to everybody in its next Room message, nobody is sent a notice, and the removal counts as something done in the room (`activity`). `Kicked` is sent by nothing else (a connection that floods the room is answered `BadRequest`), so a page that is sent it was removed by the leader, and says so. There is no ban: a Hello that shows the removed person's key is a new person's Hello (a key that fits no seat), so a page that was removed must not send the old key again, and a person who is removed and comes back is a newcomer (who is turned away with `MatchRunning` while a START waits). Budget: a burst of 3, then 1 a second; 6 more are tolerated and each one after that is a violation; a request that cannot be done (the sender does not lead, the guard is not the seating's, the seat is the leader's, a bot's or empty, a match loads) is no offence at first and counts in `ignored_removes`, each one after 16 is a violation, and the count is forgiven one a minute like the other kinds. In a room that is no lobby room a Remove is garbage.
* **SeatMove** keeps protocol 15's layout and guard. In a lobby room it exchanges the two colours **completely**: the person (all that is its own: connection, key, name, thumb, platform, place in the order of the Welcomes), what the plan calls each of the two colours and the team pair (the seats of the pair follow the exchange). A team goes with its player and a planned bot with its level, in one message that nothing can come between. A colour that a person holds is exchanged; the protocol 15 rule that a seated bot's colour is never taken is for the rooms of the control interface, which have no plan.
* No Reject reason is new. For a page they mean: `NoSuchRoom` (the server offers no lobby rooms, every one of its lobbies is in use, the server has no place for another room (`--max-rooms`) or its map is missing or cannot be played, the code has a capital letter, the Hello showed a key for a room that is gone), `Full` (no colour that the plan calls Open is free), `MatchRunning` (the leader's START waits for the games, or a match runs) and `BadRequest` (a page's Hello with a plain block, a game's with a lobby block, or a page in a room that is no lobby room).

### The lobby room

* **The door** (`RoomManager::route_hello`, `make_lobby_room`). A page's Hello with a lobby block for a code that is no room makes one when the server offers lobby rooms (`--demo-lobbies N`, which needs `--demo-rooms`, `--demo-map` and reconnect), the Hello shows **no key**, the code has **no upper-case letter** (`net::public_room_code`, the rule of public rooms) and a place of the pool is free or can be freed (below). The first Hello decides; its Welcome carries `kWelcomeCreated`. A page never makes a public room and a game never makes a lobby room: a Hello whose kind and block do not agree is `BadRequest`. A lobby room that is over (its match ended) is forgotten when a page's Hello with a lobby block comes to its code, and that Hello makes a new lobby: the way back after a match is the same code.
* **Joining.** A Hello without a key takes the colour it asked for when nobody holds it and the plan calls it Open, else the lowest such colour. Easy, Medium, Hard and Nobody colours are never taken by a joiner; no such colour is `Full`. The first player to join leads, and a lobby room has all four colours and gives every player a key. A page is seated in a lobby room only; a game can join one too (it is a game in the room's `in_game` mask).
* **Leader.** When the leader goes, the player who has been in the room the longest leads (a seat that is held counts as present, so a leader whose phone is in another app leads still); a bot never does. The Room message names the new leader; the room says nothing else (the page shows its notice).
* **The plan.** Only the leader of an open lobby room is heard, while its START does not wait.
