Every change is shown to everybody in the Room message, and the plan decides the START: the empty colours the plan gives to Easy, Medium or Hard get a bot of that level, Open and Nobody stay empty, and the plan's teams are the match's (a StartRequest's own fill and teams are not looked at). Budget: a burst of 6, then 4 a second; 12 more are tolerated and each one after that is a violation; a plan that cannot be heard (not the leader, START waits) is no offence at first and counts in `ignored_plans`, each one after 16 is a violation.
* **Hold, silence, leave.** A guest whose connection ends keeps its seat for **a minute** (`hold_ms`): its name, colour, key and place in the order of the Welcomes stay, it counts as present, its thumb is unknown, and a Hello with its key (a page or a game) takes the seat back at once. A guest that sends Leave goes at once. A connection from which **nothing at all** has come for 30 seconds (`silence_ms`; a live client answers the room's ping every second) is closed and its seat is held like any other: the link of a machine that went to sleep or died without a word cannot keep a lobby alive. The silence is not looked at while a match loads or runs, or in a room that is no lobby room.
* **START.** The leader's StartRequest is kept as a request (`kRoomStarting`) until every person is a **game**: the pages go to the game page and take their seats over with their keys (`HostLobby::take_over`, as a reload does in protocol 10), so the request waits while a seat is held or a page is still a page. While it waits nobody new is taken (`MatchRunning`), nothing moves, nothing is planned or renamed (the leader can still remove a person: a person whose game never comes need not be waited for the 90 seconds); a held seat keeps its hold. When every person is a game the room loads the plan's map, takes the plan's bots, finds a place among the public matches and starts as any leader's START does. The request ends with a notice for the leader, and the room goes on waiting, when **90 seconds** have passed and a game did not come ("<name>'s game did not come in time.", or "These games did not come in time: ..."), the map cannot be loaded, the colours cannot play the map, or the server has no place for another match; it ends quietly when its leader goes or the room could not start with whom it has left. A cancelled start (a game that cannot load the map, a leaver) ends the request, as every start does: the room waits again with everybody in it and a START asked for within the two seconds after a cancel waits them out. A lobby room never fails for cancelled starts (a room of another kind gives up after five), so it backs off instead: the first three cancels in a row cost the two seconds, every one after them a pause of **a minute**, and a START asked for in that pause is answered at once with a notice ("The match could not start a few times in a row: try again in a minute.") and does not stand, so the room is open for its plan meanwhile. The count is forgotten after fifteen minutes without a cancel.
* **Capacity.** A waiting lobby is cheap (no thread, no match), so the lobbies have a pool of their own (`--demo-lobbies N`, 200 by default) and a lobby that waits holds **no place among the `--demo-rooms`**: its START asks the server for one (a place that is free, or one that a public match which nobody has been at for a minute gives up), and the match is a public room like any other. When the pool is full (or the server has no place for another room, `--max-rooms`), the lobby that has been empty the longest is forgotten for the new one; when none is empty, the lobby in which **nothing has been done for ten minutes** (a plan, a colour move, a rename, a removal, a line of chat, a START, a newcomer: answered pings are not use), the longest idle first and on a tie the first code; its people are dropped and their pages make a room again. A lobby that is used is never given up, and when there is none of these either the Hello is `NoSuchRoom`.
* **Nobody in the room.** A lobby room has no ten minutes to fill. It is forgotten a minute (`empty_close_ms`) after the last person has gone (left, or the hold ended), **without a line in the log and with no result file** (no match was begun); a Hello for its code before that finds it, and a Hello with a lobby block after that makes a new lobby. A person who comes meanwhile starts the minute over.
* **A page never takes a seat of a match.** A page's Hello with a key for a match that loads or runs is `MatchRunning`; only a game plays: its Hello with the key takes the seat back as in protocol 10 (the pages that went to the game page do exactly that). A lobby that has started its match does not return to the lobby by itself; the page that comes back with its key finds the match, and a new Hello with a lobby block after the match makes the lobby again.
* **Restarts.** A lobby is not kept in a restart record (nothing in it is a match); a restart ends every lobby, and a page that is answered `NoSuchRoom` to its key makes the room again with a Hello that shows no key. The record of a match that a lobby started is any public room's.

### The clients

* `ClientLobby` and `NetGame` tell `kClientGame`; `ClientLobby::Config::client_kind` is what a page's test client sets. The desktop menu and the game's `--room-*` options are unchanged: neither makes a lobby block. `NetGame::way_back_hello` is a game's Hello, which is why a page's key is of no use to it.
* The decoders know the new fields: a game that joins a lobby room (a Hello to its code) sees the plan in its Room message, and is seated like any joiner; no screen of the game shows the plan yet.
* **The lobby page's client** is `web/front/lobby_net.js` (a script with no dependency, ES5, that a page loads as `AntsLobbyNet` and node as a module): the codec of every message above (each decoder refuses what the C++ one refuses), the guard of a colour move and of a removal (`seatingHash`, `seating_hash` in C++), and `LobbyClient`, which keeps the page's seat. It sends the Hello with the lobby block, answers the room's pings, takes its seat back with the key after a lost link (waits of 0.5, 1, 2, 4 and then 8 seconds; a link that has said nothing for 8 seconds is given up, the server pings every second), makes the room again with no key when the answer to its key is `NoSuchRoom` (a restart, a room that closed), and tells a screen what changed between two Room messages (joined, left, renamed, moved, swapped, host, plan, starting, the games that came in). `Kicked` is *removed* (the key is forgotten), `Superseded` is *another window has the seat*, every other `Reject` is final. The entry it makes for the game page (`rejoinEntry`: `ants.rejoin.<room>.<seat>`) is the one the game page reads, which is checked against the page's own code (`tests/scripts/web_lobby_entry_check.js`): a game page that a lobby page goes to on START finds the key of the seat, asks no name and takes the seat over. The requests (`move`, `setPlan`, `rename`, `remove`, `start`) are made only when the server would hear them (the leader, no START waiting, except that a removal is heard also then) and return false otherwise. `move` and `remove` take the guard of the screen as their last argument (`guardNow()` when the leader picks the row up or opens the question) and send nothing when the seating is not that any more, so a newcomer who took the colour meanwhile is never the one who is moved or removed. The Hello it writes says only what the server's decoder takes (a name of the printable characters of the table, at most 32 of them: "José" is sent as "Jos"; a key of 16 bytes that is not all zero; a platform byte that names a browser or another system), and a request that the server would answer with a flood rejection is not sent: the client keeps the server's own budgets (a move and a plan 6 at once and 4 a second, a name and a removal 3 at once and 1 a second) and returns false. A socket that has not opened in `connectMs` (20 s) or has said nothing for `silenceMs` (8 s) is given up and the way back is tried (a link that stayed up for `stableMs`, 10 s, starts the waits at 0.5 s again); `wake()` does the same at once when a page comes back to the front. `leave()` gives the seat up at once, also between the Hello and the Welcome (the server has the person by then; a tab that closes without it holds the colour for a minute). Every Room message says which colour is the page's own, so a page that the leader moved knows its seat and its name; `rejoinEntry()` is the entry for that seat, which a page writes when it hears START before it goes to the game page (it never calls `leave()` then), and the hand-over check moves a page before START and proves that the game takes the colour the page was moved to. A handler that throws does not stop the client or the other handlers (the error goes to `onError`, else to the console), the key never travels in an event, and a page shows a name as text, never as markup. `web/lobby.html` loads it.

### Limits

* Whoever reaches a game port can fill the pool and keep it filled with connections that stay (nothing behind the site's nginx tells one address from another); that is the price of a page that needs no secret, as for the public rooms. What bounds it: a lobby in which nothing is done for ten minutes gives its place up when it is needed, and one that nobody is in goes after a minute. Nothing bounds how fast one address makes lobbies (a lobby costs about 0.3 ms to make, reading the map file, against 0.03 ms for a Hello that is refused, and making one is what every page view does); a limit by address belongs in front of the server, and the site's nginx has none for the game's WebSocket yet (behind a reverse proxy every visitor may share its address). The people in lobbies are counted in `GET /busy` as `players`, never as `matches`, so lobbies never hold a deploy back.
* Only the leader can remove a person (Remove, above): there is no vote and no ban (a removed person who opens the link again is a new person). A person who has joined and whose game never comes makes the START end after 90 seconds with a notice that names them, unless the leader removes them first.
* A lobby is lost when the server restarts, and so are its plan and its people; the page makes a new one.
* The plan of a lobby room is the leader's: there is no vote and no colour picker for anybody else.
* A held seat looks like a present player to the others for a minute (a player who closed the tab is still shown); no screen draws it differently yet.
* `valid_create_block` accepts teams in a lobby block; the server ignores them and the plan decides.

### Tests

`test_lockstep` **N2.105** (the Hello's client kind, the lobby block and the Welcome's created bit), **N2.106** (the Plan: every kind, every pair of teams, every refusal), **N2.107** (the Room's plan, games and rule bits, and what the decoder refuses), **N2.108** (the Name: every character, every refusal), **N2.109** (the words of the server's sentences, which the front page prints as they are) **N2.110** (the Remove: the layout, every refusal) and **N2.111** (`tests/data/lobby_messages.txt`: the bytes that the encoders write for what a lobby page sends and hears; the page's codec is held to the same file by `tests/scripts/web_lobby_net_check.js`); `test_lobby` **N4.26** (the joiners), **N4.27** (the plan), **N4.28** (a colour move that exchanges the colours completely), **N4.29** (the hold), **N4.30** (START waits for the games, and how it ends), **N4.31** (what a Hello is: a page, a game, the created bit), **N4.32** (the silence), **N4.33** (the name: who is heard, the bot-like names, the guard, the budget), **N4.34** (a lobby room forgives a guest one violation and one ignored request of each kind every minute), **N4.35** (a flood of STARTs), **N4.36** (what counts as something done in a room, for its idle clock) and **N4.37** (the removal: who is heard, the guard, a held seat, a START that waits, the budget); `test_server` **S3.132** (lobbies are off unless the server offers them, and what a Hello needs), **S3.133** (the plan through a real room), **S3.134** (START waits for every game), **S3.135** (the plan's bots and teams are the match's), **S3.136** (a START that cannot be carried out), **S3.137** (a lobby closes when nobody is in it), **S3.138** (the pool), **S3.139** (a page never takes a seat of a match; the lobby of a finished one), **S3.152** (a lobby room from the control interface's door), **S3.153** (a START that is cancelled again and again: the back-off), **S3.154** (a rename through a real room, and the name of the match), **S3.155** (two Hellos in one pass), **S3.156** (the 32-bit clock), **S3.157** (idle lobbies give their places up), **S3.158** (a tie between empty lobbies), **S3.159** (a lobby that was idle for longer than the 32-bit clock counts is still idle), **S3.160** (a room's idle time, read from the room itself; a match that a lobby began is never given up as an idle lobby), **S3.161** (the records that wait for their replay count as rooms when a new lobby needs a place) and **S3.162** (a removal through a real room, also while a START waits); and the page's client against a scripted server and a fake browser, `tests/scripts/web_lobby_client_check.js`, and its entry against the game page's reader, `tests/scripts/web_lobby_entry_check.js` (`tests/scripts/test_web_lobby_net.py` runs them and the codec check).

## Reconnect (protocol 10): a player whose connection is lost can come back

Request of the owner: "if a user disconnects can we give them some time to reconnect? maybe ask the other players if they want to continue without the player after some time?", then "if your browser crashed or your power went out, you could communicate with those players and they could hold the game paused until you get back". The original has no pause, no vote and no way back (5.47 of `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md`: a peer that is gone is dropped, the others read "%s dropped out of the game!"). **Release A** (protocol 10, the server side behind a switch: a room holds seats only when it is told to, so nothing changes for players) **shipped in v0.0.98**; **release B** adds the clients and turns it on: **`NetGame`'s part is built** (the library comes back to its match by itself: "What the clients do in release B"), the application's screens and the key storage are built too (the same section), and **the switch is on**: `kReconnectByDefault` is true, so every room of a server holds seats unless the server is started with `--no-reconnect` or the room says `"reconnect": false`. This section says what exists and what is left.

**Restart records:** the server also keeps the matches of its rooms across a restart of the server itself: see "Restart records" below (no new protocol number; `NetGame` rejoins by itself and the application shows it). **Status: the server side is complete, and on by default since release B** (`--no-reconnect` turns it off for a server, a room's `"reconnect": false` for one room). What is in: protocol 10 (the messages below), `TurnLog` (the server's log of every turn, bounded per room and by a budget that all rooms share), `Attendance` (the rules of the pause, the vote and the cap), `LockstepRunner::fast_forward` (the catch-up without a hook, the jitter buffer left alone), the lobby's keys, **`HostSession`** (holds the seat of a lost connection and pauses the match, tells the players who is missing, takes a returning connection over, streams the log to it, compares its state hash with the referee's and gives the seat back), **`ClientSession`** (a lost link is not the end: new link, Hello with the key, the match again, the server's word; a Reject ends it), and the server around them: the room's keys and settings, the door that takes a Hello with a key to the running room, the status with its counters, the control interface's `reconnect`, `hold_vote_seconds`, `max_pause_seconds`, `max_catch_up_seconds` and `resume_countdown_seconds`, the options `--reconnect`, `--no-reconnect`, `--hold-vote-seconds`, `--max-pause-seconds`, `--max-catch-up-seconds`, `--resume-countdown-seconds` and `--log-mb`, and the budget for the logs. **After the independent review of release A** (its findings are one section below, "What a key holder can do to a room") a seat holder has budgets (a catch-up time per absence, three Hellos a minute, three times the log's size in ten minutes), the pause cap is absolute, a seat that flaps is put to the vote, the clients' runners forget a pause, a log that is dead frees its memory at once, a client gives up by the room's cap, and the match is held for a **resume countdown** after a pause. **The game's own clients** were not in release A: `NetGame` now uses `ClientSession`'s reconnect when a room hands it a key ("What the clients do in release B", below); the application's screens and the key storage came with release B, which turned the switch on. Everything about LAN and direct games is unchanged.

### The behaviour that is decided

* **Only a LOST connection pauses the match.** A seat is *lost* when its link closes, a send to it fails, or **nothing at all arrives from it for 10 s** (`silence_ms`: a power cut or a frozen machine leaves a half-open link that only silence reveals; a live client sends an acknowledgement every 50 ms and a ping a second). A player that is slow but keeps talking (a hidden tab, a laptop that is catching up) is *lag*, never a pause: the server keeps sealing, the others are told "Bob is lagging" (protocol 8), and the lag policy drops it at 60 s behind or after 30 s without progress. A room that does not hold seats, and a LAN game, keep today's rules unchanged.
* **While a seat is missing nothing is sealed: the match is paused for everybody.** Chat goes on between the players who are there; commands are discarded without counting as violations (a pause is long). The others are told who is missing and for how long (the overlay, release B, appears after one second of pause: a blip of a few hundred milliseconds is not worth a banner). **After a pause of 3 s or more the match stays held for a resume countdown** (10 s by default, a room setting `resume_countdown_seconds` 0 - 60, 0 = none; the owner's request: "instead of instantly restarting"): the referee seals nothing, Presence carries the seconds left (`resume_s`, at its start and once a second), chat is relayed and commands are discarded as in a pause, a seat that is lost during it cancels it (the pause rules apply and a countdown follows that pause, however short), a shorter pause (a blip) resumes at once. **The countdown is no pause**: it does not count toward the 30-minute cap or anybody's catch-up time, and the wall-clock limit leaves it out like a pause.
* **The seat comes back with its key** (issued with the Welcome of the seat, kept by the client: the web page in `localStorage`, the game in a file, release B) and is given the match from the server's turn log, executes it at once without drawing it, reports its state hash, and the server resumes when the hash agrees with its own.
* **The others decide when to stop waiting.** After **30 s away in all** (the seat's total over the match, each loss counting at least 5 s) the connected players may vote "Keep waiting" / "Continue without `name`"; **more than half of the connected players** choosing "Continue" drops the seat (the existing `Drop`, sealed in one turn, the same tick everywhere; with two players the survivor wins, as the engine already does). **A seat that is lost three times within a minute FLAPS and is put to the vote at once, whatever its state** (missing, catching up, or back), with the votes kept across its returns. **There is no automatic drop for a seat's own time**: they wait as long as they want, within a **cap on the match's total paused time** (30 minutes by default, a room setting `max_pause_seconds`, 60 - 86,400; every pause counts at least 5 s, so a connection that flaps reaches it): **the cap is absolute: at the cap every seat that is not present is dropped**, one that is catching up and shows progress too. A key holder has budgets too (below). If every remaining player leaves, the room ends as it does today.

### The messages (protocol 10)

`kProtocolVersion` was 10 when this section was written (9 was the community-map rules of the lock-step simulation, which changed no message; **11** changed the StartRequest and gave the room's notices to Chat: "Protocol 11", above; every layout below is still the layout of protocol 11). A Hello of any older protocol is answered `VersionMismatch` before its layout is read (`decode_hello_prefix` reads the version and the name, which lead every layout), so the Hello of protocols 6 - 9 (no key and no turns after the token) is told so by a LAN host and by a server alike. All values little endian; every decoder checks every length, count and range and refuses a trailing byte (fuzzed with 400,000 random and mutated messages, N2.3).

| message | type | direction | layout (after the type byte) | rules the decoder enforces |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hello (changed) | 1 | client -> host | the fields of protocol 6 (`u16 version, str8 name, u16 listen_port, u8 want_seat, str8 room, str8 token`), then **`u8[16] key`, `u32 have_turns`** | key zero = a new player; `have_turns` is 0 without a key (a count belongs to a key); it says how many turns the machine has executed (0: it starts from nothing and is sent Start first) |
| Welcome (changed) | 2 | host -> client | `u8 player, u8 players`, then **`u8[16] key, u8 flags`** | key zero = this room offers no way back (a LAN host, a room that does not hold seats); `flags` 0 or 1 (bit 0 `kWelcomeRejoin`: a Hello with a key was accepted into a match that runs, Start or CatchUp follow, no Room message does) and 1 needs a key |
| Reject (changed) | 3 | host -> client | `u8 reason` | 1 - 6 as before; **7 Dropped** (the key is right, the seat was dropped: the original's string 94), **8 RejoinFailed** (the way back is closed: the server's log is not usable, it has no Start to tell a machine that has nothing how to load the match, that machine could not load the map, **or the seat has used up what a key holder may ask of the server: the catch-up time of the absence, three Hellos a minute, three times the log's size streamed in ten minutes**; on the machine's side a stream that does not fit its announcement or the server's `Desync` verdict end the session with this reason; the seat stays held: the others may vote and the cap applies), **9 Superseded** (a newer connection with the key took the seat; sent to the older one just before it is closed so that its window stops trying) |
| **Presence** | 26 | server -> the players, per recipient | `u8 n (0 .. 4)`, `n x { u8 seat, u8 state (1 absent, 2 catching up), u16 waited_s, u8 progress }`, `u8 vote_seat (255 none), u8 votes_continue, u8 voters, u8 your_vote (0 none, 1 keep waiting, 2 continue), u16 cap_s`, **`u8 resume_s`** | seats distinct and below 4; progress 0 .. 100 and 0 for an absent seat; `waited_s` not growing along the list (longest away first); `vote_seat` is 255 or **a seat (below 4) in any state**: the absent seat that has been away longest, once it is put to the vote, or a seat that FLAPS, missing or back; `votes_continue <= voters <= 4`; `your_vote <= 2`; without a vote `votes_continue` and `your_vote` are 0; **`resume_s` is 0 .. 60 and 0 while a seat is missing**. `n = 0`: nobody is missing: the match runs, or `resume_s > 0`: it is held for the countdown that follows a pause (the seconds left, rounded up). `waited_s` is the seat's total away time in seconds (saturates at 0xFFFF); `cap_s` is the seconds of pause that the match has left before the cap (rounded up, so 0 exactly when the cap is reached; 0xFFFF = 65,535 s or more); `voters` are the connected players that may vote (never the seat that the vote is about). The server sends it on every change (a seat lost or back, a vote cast, a vote that opens or closes) and once a second while the match is held (`HostSession`) |
| **Vote** | 27 | player -> server | `u8 seat, u8 choice (0 keep waiting, 1 continue without it)` | 3 bytes; seat below 4. A vote that does not fit (no vote is open, the seat is not its subject, the voter is not connected) is ignored by the server, no violation: it crossed a state change |
| **CatchUp** | 28 | server -> a player who came back | `u32 first_turn, u32 total_turns` | 9 bytes; `first_turn <= total_turns`; the stream ends with turn `total_turns - 1`; equal = the player has everything |
| **TurnBatch** | 29 | server -> a player who came back | `u32 first_turn, u16 count (1 .. 4096)`, then `count x { u16 commands (<= 512), the commands in their wire form }` | consecutive turns from `first_turn` in canonical order, issuers stamped; `first_turn + count` may not pass 2^32; the whole message is at most 64 KB (`kMaxMessageBytes`) and the server fills one up to 48 KB (`kBatchBytes`, after the first turn: a turn that is alone bigger is a batch of its own). `encode_turn_batch_packed` makes the same bytes from turns that are packed already (the log keeps them so) |
| **CaughtUp** | 30 | the player -> server | `u32 turns`, then the 8 `u64` of `StateHash` | 69 bytes; `turns` must equal `total_turns` and the hash is the state after exactly that many turns (the server checks both; a different hash is answered with a `Desync` to that client alone and the seat stays away) |

`MsgType::Last` is `CaughtUp`. The 20-turn `Hash` report and `TurnAck` of the live match are as before; `Chat` is relayed during a pause. `key_matches` compares two keys in constant time and never calls a zero key equal to anything.

### The keys and the waiting room (`HostLobby`, `ClientLobby`)

`HostLobby::Config::make_key` (a `std::function<bool(SeatKey&)>`; unset = no keys, as a LAN host) gives a guest a key at its Welcome; the server passes the operating system's generator (`ants::server::random_bytes`, the one that makes the control secret; `ants_net` never reads the operating system's generator itself, it is built for the web too). A key that is zero, equals another seat's, or a maker that fails gives that guest no key (the zero key in its Welcome; four tries first). `key_of(seat)` is what the match's session will be handed. **A Hello with the key of a seated guest while the room is open takes the seat over** (`takeovers()` counts them): same seat, key, name and **place in the order of the Welcomes** (so the leader stays the leader, and when the leader goes the same guest follows), its violations and message budget (a connection that is thrown out cannot start again by coming back), the thumb is measured again, the old connection is told `Superseded` and closed, the room is broadcast; the room code is checked first, and a key beats the room being full. A key that fits no seat is the Hello of a new player (a wrong key reveals nothing); the key of a seat that was left is dead (no tombstone: a page whose old link was seen to be closed before it comes back joins as a new player). In a match that loads or runs the lobby answers `MatchRunning` to a key, as to any Hello: the session of the match is the one that takes a seat back (`HostSession::accept_rejoin`, below). `ClientLobby::Config::key` makes its Hello show the key (its `have_turns` is 0: it starts from nothing); `key()` and `rejoined()` read what the Welcome said; Welcome(rejoin) -> Start -> `report_loaded` -> Begin -> Begun is the same flow as for anybody.

### The turn log (`turnlog.hpp`)

The server keeps every sealed turn so that a player who comes back can be given the whole match: packed as it travels (`u16 command count` and the commands in their wire form, one blob and an index of 32-bit offsets, so that any range can be cut out and sent as it is). An empty turn costs 2 + 4 bytes. With turns of 50 ms a match of 30 minutes has **36,000 turns**: an order-free match is 216,000 bytes (the index is 144,000 of them), busy play (a generated stream of 4 - 5 orders a second, up to 24 ants each) **651,372 bytes** in memory (N2.41), cut into 11 batches of 48 KB. **A hostile client can make it huge**: the sequencer takes 64 commands per player and turn, 8.7 KB of 32 ants each, 35 KB a turn for four players, **1.25 GB in 30 minutes**. So a log has a limit (16 MiB per room, `kDefaultMaxBytes`; never more than `0xFFFFFFF0` bytes whatever limit it is given): the append that would pass it stores nothing and **the log is dead from then on** (a log with a hole serves no replay; the room falls back to dropping a lost seat at once), as is one that is given a turn out of order, a turn too big for a message, or the growth that the server's budget refuses. **A dead log frees what it held at the moment it dies** (`turns()` and `bytes()` are 0 from then on, and the budget has its bytes back; it used to keep them to the end of the match, so that 16 hostile rooms made the server's way back unavailable to every room that came after them): nobody can be streaming from it then, because a log only grows while the match runs and a seat is only brought back while it is paused. **The budget counts what a log has allocated (`capacity_bytes()`: the capacity of its two vectors, which grow by doubling and hold up to twice what they store), not what it stores**; the first allocation is 1.5 KiB (1 KiB of turns and 128 index entries: a budget of less keeps no log), and an append whose doubling the budget refuses tries a growth of a quarter before the log dies, so that a log with the last bytes of the budget does not copy itself at every turn. `bytes_between(from, to)` measures a range (what a stream window counts in bytes: a WebSocket fails at 1 MB of backlog), `read(from, max_turns, limit_bytes, out)` cuts a batch (at least one turn).

### The attendance rules (`attendance.hpp`)

Per seat of a person: **Present, Absent** (lost: held, key valid, match paused), **CatchingUp** (back, being given the turns: still paused) and **Dropped** (final). The session says what it sees; `Attendance` decides.

| event | from -> to | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| `lost(seat)` | Present -> Absent | the absence begins (its catch-up time starts again); the loss is counted for the flapping rule; the seat's own votes are cleared, and the votes about it too unless it flaps |
| `returning(seat, planned_bytes, log_bytes)` | Absent, CatchingUp, Present (a second window: a loss too) -> CatchingUp | **only if the seat's budgets allow it** (below; otherwise nothing changes and the refusal is counted); the away time goes on from the loss (a Present seat's starts now); **a Hello of an attempt that is in progress changes nothing of it** (stall clock, percent, start) |
| `progress(seat, percent)` | CatchingUp | only a **higher** percent than the last is a sign of life and restarts the stall clock (a client cannot hold the pause by repeating an acknowledgement) |
| `caught_up(seat)` | CatchingUp -> Present | the hashes agreed; the absence counts **at least `min_absence_ms` (5 s)** toward the seat's total; its catch-up time is given back |
| `catch_up_failed(seat)` | CatchingUp -> Absent | the link closed or the hash differed; the away time kept running and the attempt's time is spent from the absence's catch-up time |
| `dropped(seat)` | any -> Dropped | the seat left or was thrown out: final, nothing brings it back, every later call is a no-op |
| `update(now)` | | a catch-up with **no progress for 20 s** (`catch_up_stall_ms`) **or whose absence has used its catch-up time** (`max_catch_up_ms`, 5 minutes) is let go (Absent again); a seat that stopped flapping closes its vote; a **won vote** drops its seat; when the match's pauses have used up **`max_pause_ms`** **every seat that is not Present is dropped, Absent or CatchingUp** (a later loss is dropped by the next update: the match has no pause left). Returns the seats that were dropped: the session seals their `Drop`s |

* **Away time is the seat's total over the match** (the absences that are over, each at least 5 s, plus the one that goes on), so a connection that flaps every second is put to the vote after six losses, not thirty.
* **One vote at a time**, about the Absent seat with the longest total (the lowest seat of a tie) once it has been away 30 s (`vote_after_ms`), **or about a seat that flaps**. The vote is won when `2 * votes > connected`, connected being the Present seats of persons (not bots, not seats that are away, **not the seat itself**: a seat does not vote on its own case): 1 of 1, 2 of 2, 2 of 3 (a match has four seats, so a missing one leaves at most three voters); a player who does not vote counts as not wanting to continue; a changed choice counts; a voter that is lost leaves the count at once and its choice is gone for good; votes about a seat are cleared when it changes state (not for a seat that flaps); a vote only counts while its seat is the subject. A seat that is catching up is not a subject, unless it flaps.
* **FLAPPING (review fix, MEDIUM).** A seat whose connection is lost **three times within 60 s** (`kFlapLosses`, `kFlapWindowMs`; a second window that takes over a seat that was Present counts as a loss) flaps: it is the subject of the vote **at once** (no 30 s of away time needed), **in any state: missing, catching up, or back and Present**, and its votes stay across its returns until the vote is decided (the others drop it, even though it is connected: its link is closed and told `Dropped`) or until 60 s pass without another loss, which closes the vote. Before, a seat that came back every 300 ms kept the match paused 72 % of the time (34 % speed) and could not be voted out, because the vote was cleared at every return and a seat that was catching up or back was no subject.
* **The cap** is on the match's **total paused time** (any seat Absent or CatchingUp, each moment counted once, **and every pause counted at least 5 s**, so a connection that flaps reaches it): 30 minutes by default, `cap_s` tells the screens how many seconds are left. **It is absolute** (review fix, HIGH): when it is reached every seat that is not Present is dropped at once, a seat that is catching up and shows progress too (it used to keep its chance for as long as it showed progress, and a key holder that said Hello again and again held a room paused for ever); a seat that went absent after it is dropped as before.
* **The budgets of a key holder** (review fixes, HIGH). A key is a bearer secret, so what it may ask of the room and of the server is bounded: **(1) the catch-up time of one absence**, 5 minutes by default (`max_catch_up_ms`, a room setting `max_catch_up_seconds` 10 - 3600), over **all the attempts** of the absence: when it is spent `update()` fails the catch-up at that moment (the seat is Absent: the vote and the cap apply) and a Hello is refused; a Hello of an attempt that is in progress does not start the stall clock, the percent or the time again (a Hello every 12 s held a room with a cap of 60 s paused for 176 s, with no drop by the cap and no vote); catching up, or a new loss, gives the time back; **(2) twelve Hellos a minute per seat** (three until the owner asked for four times as many, so that switching the page's picture a few times in a minute keeps the seat; the most a Config may ask for is 16) (`rejoin_attempts`, `rejoin_window_ms`), the window sliding to the millisecond, and **(3) three times the log's size streamed to one seat in ten minutes, at least 1 MiB** (`stream_factor`, `stream_window_ms`, `stream_floor_bytes`; the stream is charged as it is sent, in ten groups of a minute; the floor is so that a log of a few KB is no reason to refuse the fifth reload of a page): beyond either the Hello is answered **`RejoinFailed`**, the seat stays as it was (Absent: the vote and the cap apply), nothing of an attempt that may be in progress is touched, and the refusal is counted (`rejoins_refused` in the status) and costs the seat nothing. A client that gets `RejoinFailed` ends for good (release B decides whether it backs off); a flapping link of a legitimate player that says Hello more than three times a minute gets it too: that is the price of the bound.
* **THE RESUME COUNTDOWN** (the owner's request). When a pause ends (a seat is back and verified, or a vote or the cap dropped the last seat that was missing) after at least 3 s (`resume_min_pause_ms`) the match stays held for `resume_countdown_ms` (10 s by default, a room setting, 0 = none; **the library's default is none**, a room's is ten seconds): `counting_down(now)`, `resume_s(now)` (the seconds left, rounded up), nothing is sealed (the session's `paused()` is true: commands discarded, chat relayed, bots wait, the lag clocks stand still). **It is not a pause**: `pause_ms` (what the cap counts) and the catch-up time do not include it; `held_ms(now)` (the pauses as they really lasted and the countdowns) is what the wall-clock limit leaves out. A loss cancels it (the time it ran is kept in `held_ms`) and **a countdown follows that pause, however short**.
* All times are 32-bit milliseconds of the host's clock, used as differences only (`clock.hpp`: they wrap after 49.7 days); the tests run the same scenario from eight different clocks, among them 8 s before the wrap and at the signed half, and get the same decisions at the same offsets.
* `presence_for(viewer, now)` builds the Presence message of the state; a random walk of 16,000 events over every call checks that the message is always one that the decoder accepts and that says what the state says (N2.44).

### The catch-up (`LockstepRunner::fast_forward`)

The turns of a stream are handed in with `on_catch_up_turn` and run with `fast_forward(max_ticks)`: as many as the caller allows at once, **no presentation hook** (nothing is drawn, no sound, no news: the past is only replayed; the one hook that is called is `set_on_executed`, the watcher of every turn that runs, which the replay recorder is), in exactly the order of `update()` (a turn's commands in order, then its tick; a turn is one tick, so there is no half-done turn: `at_boundary()` is true except inside the hooks of a paced `update`). The state after every turn is the paced runner's (600 turns of the four-player match, in slices of every kind: N2.45; a turn with an invitation and its acceptance proves the order). **The catch-up does not feed the adaptive jitter buffer**: its turns all arrive at once, "late" by minutes, and would make the buffer ask for its maximum, and the lateness measured before the pause describes a link and a clock that are gone. A fast-forward that ran any turn leaves the runner as at the start of a match: `JitterBuffer::reset()` (the steady target, no turn read), the next live turns collected before the first of them runs (target + 1 are queued first), and the first update after it does not count the live turns that waited for it (N2.46: a rough link before the pause does not make the buffer grow after it). A fast-forward of an empty queue changes nothing. Speed (research for the design, on an Apple M4, not repeated here): 36,000 turns replay in 0.5 - 11 s natively and 0.8 - 15 s as WebAssembly, depending on how much the players do (the path searches are what costs); native and WebAssembly end in the same state hash.

### A pause is no stall of the link (`LockstepRunner::set_held`, review fix MEDIUM)

The others' runners used to read a pause as a late turn: the queue ran dry, the runner stalled, and when the turns came again **the jitter buffer grew for a pause that was nobody's link** (one rejoin pause of 130 - 210 ms took the others' buffers from 1 to 2 to 4 turns, about 150 ms more input delay for 30 s, in every other player's window; `reset_after_ms` forgets samples only for stalls of 1.5 s or more, and a stall of up to 100 ms *grows* the buffer). Now **the client tells its runner what Presence says**: while a seat is missing, or the countdown after a pause runs, the runner is *held* (`ClientSession::apply_presence` -> `set_held(true)`). A held runner runs the turns it has (the server sealed them), then waits **without a stall** (no "Waiting for the other players...", nothing told to the jitter buffer, `stalled_ms()` stays 0 for a pause of 30 s) and collects the buffer again for the turns that follow; **when the pause ends (`set_held(false)`) the lateness that was read before it is forgotten** (the first turn after a pause of 150 ms is 150 ms "late" for every turn that follows) but the buffer itself is not reset (a rough link keeps the three or four turns it needs: N2.79). The same is done for the countdown, which is also a time in which no turn comes. Measured with the review's driver (`drv_jit`, three cuts of one seat of three, links of 5 and of 20 ms): the others' buffers stay at **1** after every cut and 12 s later (they were 2, then 4).

### The sessions (`session.hpp`): what `HostSession` and `ClientSession` do

**`HostSession`** (a host without a seat, the referee of a server's room; `Config::hold_seats`, off by default: every other host, a game on the local network above all, is exactly what it was). The seat's key comes from the room (`set_seat_keys`, copied from `HostLobby::key_of`); a seat without one (a bot's, an empty one, a guest that was given none) cannot be held.

* **Lost or dropped.** A connection is **lost** at the three places where the link is gone: `poll_clients` sees it closed, a `send` to it fails (`broadcast`), or **nothing at all arrived from it for `silence_ms` = 10 s** (a half-open link: a power cut, a frozen machine, a Wi-Fi switch). `lose()` holds the seat (`Attendance::lost`, the connection closed, the seat made inactive in the sequencer so that it gates nothing, a Presence to the others) when the room holds seats, the log is usable and the seat has a key, and otherwise is `drop()`: the seat is gone for good at once, as before there was a way back. A **drop** also comes from `Leave`, from eight violations and from the lag policy (a connected client 60 s behind or with no acknowledgement for 30 s: the rules of protocol 8 are unchanged). `Attendance::dropped` is final, and **keys survive the drop**: whoever shows the key of a dropped seat is told `Dropped`, not "the match has started".
* **`update(now)`, in this order:** `poll_clients` (may lose); the silence check (lose); `Attendance::update` (a catch-up that went nowhere is let go, a vote that was won, the cap: each seat that it returns is dropped: `finish_drop` seals its `Drop` and tells a returning player of that seat `Dropped`, **and a connected one too: a seat that flaps and is voted out while it is back has its link closed**); `pump_rejoiners`; **Presence** when anything changed, when the held state changed (a pause, and the countdown after it) or a vote opened or closed, and **once a second while the match is held** (a seat that flaps is put to the vote while the match runs: told when the vote opens, at every vote and when it closes) (`Attendance::presence_for(viewer)`: each player is told its own vote); the sealing loop, **which seals nothing while the match is held** (`paused()`: a seat is Absent or CatchingUp, **or the resume countdown that follows a pause runs**; `next_seal_ms_` slides to now, as it does behind a laggard, so no burst of turns follows) and appends every sealed turn to the `TurnLog` **before** it broadcasts it; the lag policy (**skipped while the match is held**: nobody can fall behind in a pause, and the clocks of "30 s without an ack" start again afterwards); the referee's own runner.
* **During a pause**: `Chat` is relayed as always; `Command` is **discarded without a violation** (a pause is long, and a player who goes on clicking would otherwise reach the sequencer's limit of 64 commands per turn, which no turn resets, and be thrown out by eight violations: an innocent player dropped by a pause; a command that cannot be decoded is still a violation); `Vote` goes to `Attendance::vote` (one that does not fit crossed a state change: ignored, no offence; a `Vote` to a host that does not hold seats is a violation, like any message that such a host never receives: N2.69); a bot's orders (`submit_bot`) wait too.
* **`accept_rejoin(conn, hello, now)`** is what the room's door calls for a Hello with a key. The key decides the seat (all four are compared, in constant time: `key_matches`; a zero key never matches); **no seat has this key, or the session does not hold seats: `MatchRunning`**, exactly what a Hello without a key is told, so a wrong key reveals nothing; the seat is Dropped: **`Dropped`**; the log is not usable, or the machine has no turns and the session has no Start message to give it: `RejoinFailed`; more turns than were ever sealed: `BadRequest`. **The seat's budgets are asked first** (`Attendance::returning`: the catch-up time of the absence, three Hellos a minute, three times the log's size: beyond any of them `RejoinFailed`, and nothing of the seat or of an attempt in progress is touched; the bytes asked are what a stream from `have_turns` would take, the stream is charged as it is sent). A seat whose old connection is still open (a half-dead link, a second window with the same key) is **taken over: the old connection is told `Superseded` and closed**, and so is an earlier attempt of the same seat that is still catching up. Then `Welcome{key, flags = 1}` and, for a machine that has no turns, the room's `Start`, or, for one that has some, `CatchUp{have_turns, total}` at once. The returning connection is **not a client of the host** until it has caught up (a `Rejoiner`: its own flood budget and violations, its own stage).
* **`pump_rejoiners`.** `Loaded{ok}` (a machine that started from nothing says that it loaded the map) is answered with `Begin` and `CatchUp{0, total}`, `ok = false` with `Reject RejoinFailed`; **the stream** (charged to the seat's byte budget as it is sent) reads batches from the log (`TurnLog::read`: at most 4096 turns and 48 KB) **while the bytes between what the machine has executed and what was sent are fewer than `stream_window_bytes` = 256 KB** (a WebSocket fails a connection at 1 MB of backlog, and a hostile log of 35 KB turns would reach that within 30 turns of a window counted in turns); `TurnAck` is what the machine has executed (never more than was sent) and, as a **higher percent only**, a sign of life for `Attendance::progress`; `Ping` is answered; `Leave` is final; `Command`, `Hash`, `Chat` and `Vote` from a connection that has just said `CaughtUp` are no offence; everything else counts toward eight violations. **`CaughtUp{turns, hash}`**: `turns` must be the announced total; the referee compares the hash as soon as its runner has executed every sealed turn at a boundary with an empty queue. **A runner that has not started (fewer turns queued than its buffer asks for: a seat lost in the first 50 ms) or whose buffer was just rebuilt keeps up to `buffer` turns for the next ones, which do not come while the match waits: the referee runs its own queue (`fast_forward`) for the comparison**, or such a rejoin could never finish (N2.70). Equal hashes: the connection becomes the seat's (`Attendance::caught_up`, the seat active in the sequencer, level with it), a Presence tells everybody that the seat is back and, when it was the last one, that the match runs; a different hash: **a `Desync` to that machine only** (it is not in `desyncs()`, the room does not fail, the others are not told) and the seat stays away: the attempt is over.
* **`uses_connection(c)`**: the session points at the connections of its clients (also the last one of a seat that is held) and of its returning players; the room asks before it frees one (below).

**`ClientSession`** (`Config::reconnect`, off by default; it needs the key). A lost link is no longer the end: a closed link or a host that is silent for `host_silence_ms` (10 s) with a key and `reconnect` is **`Mode::Reconnecting`** (the give-up clock starts: **the last cap that a Presence named, `cap_s`, and a minute** - counted from the loss, or from that Presence when a seat was missing then, the pause being on already; a cap of "0xFFFF, or more" is a day; **31 minutes (`reconnect_give_up_ms`) before any Presence**: the default cap and a minute; `give_up_ms()` says; `lost()` follows when it passes, or at once on a `Reject`). `wants_connection(now)` asks the owner (`NetGame`) for a new link, at once and then 2 s after the last attempt began (`reconnect_every_ms`); `attach(link, now)` hands it over (`attach(nullptr)`: no link could be made) and the session is **`Rejoining`**: **the Hello goes out when the link is OPEN** (a TCP link made without blocking is Connecting for a while: a Hello written to it then is lost; found by the prototype) with `have_turns = next_turn_expected()` (the turns it has received, run or not) and the key; no Welcome in `rejoin_timeout_ms` (10 s) abandons the attempt. `Welcome` with the rejoin flag makes it **`CatchingUp`**: `CatchUp` (its first turn must be the next one expected) and `TurnBatch` (consecutive, never beyond the announced total) go into `LockstepRunner::on_catch_up_turn`; each `update` runs `fast_forward(catch_up_ticks)` (the owner may lower it to slice the work into frames); the machine **acknowledges when its queue is empty and at every higher percent** (so the stream's window opens and the server sees progress), pings once a second, and when it has run every announced turn at a boundary with an empty queue sends **`CaughtUp{total, state hash}`** and **waits for the server's word** (the first `Presence` or live `Turn`) before it is `Normal` again: it gives no command meanwhile. A machine that starts from nothing (a page that was reloaded) does the Hello with its `ClientLobby` (key in the Hello: Welcome with the rejoin flag, `Start`, `Loaded`, `Begin`) and starts its session with `rejoin = true`, in `CatchingUp`; a server that sends `Start` to a session that has no turns is answered `Loaded{ok}` (the map is the match's own). **Any `Reject` in any mode** (the seat was dropped, the room is gone, a newer window took it) ends the session at once with `reject_reason()`; a link that closes in the middle of an attempt is `Reconnecting` again, the give-up clock keeps its first start, and the next Hello says how many turns the machine has by then. Protocol failures (a Welcome without the rejoin flag, a stream that does not fit its announcement, the server's `Desync` verdict) end it with `RejoinFailed`. `submit` needs `Normal` and **a match that is not held** (the last Presence names no seat that is missing and no countdown that runs: the host would discard it; `paused()` says, `resume_seconds_left()` is the seconds for the overlay); `vote` and `chat` need `Normal`; `leave()` ends the attempts.

**A silent server is silent in real time, and only when it was asked** (`note_gap(gap_ms)`: the rule that the work for hidden browser tabs introduced for a page that is not woken, which the way back builds on). A page that the browser does not wake gives the session at most a second of clock per wake-up and hands the rest of the gap over after the link was read; a host that said nothing in that update has been silent for the gap too. Only a host that has been **asked** (a ping went out in an earlier update and nothing at all has come since: `asked_`, `asked_ms_`) is held to all of it; one that was not asked yet gets the gap only up to `kAskGraceMs` (3 s) short of the 10 s, so that the ping the wake-up sends is the question and its answer, a message, wakes the page (2.9 s after the question: not silent, 3.1 s: silent; a live server that answers its pings, or has data waiting, is never condemned, whatever the gaps: N2.74). The same rule serves every mode that waits for the server: Normal and CatchingUp (the pings are the questions; the silence takes a machine to `Reconnecting`, or ends an attempt), and, counted in full, Rejoining (the Hello is the question: no Welcome in 10 s of real time abandons the attempt) and Reconnecting (the next attempt 2 s after the last, the give-up after 31 minutes). The caller judges the session at once afterwards (`update` with the same clock), as it does after every update.

### The server around it (`room.hpp`, `room_manager.hpp`, `control.hpp`, `main.cpp`)

* **The switch.** `RoomSpec::reconnect` (the control interface's `"reconnect"`, else the server's `--reconnect` / `--no-reconnect`: **on by default since release B**, `kReconnectByDefault` in `room_manager.hpp`; a `RoomSpec` that code builds says false unless it is told otherwise) makes the room's lobby give every player a key (`ants::server::random_bytes`: the operating system's generator; `ants_net` itself never reads it) and the match's session hold seats (`hold_seats`, the keys, the room's Start message, the vote time `vote_after_ms`, the cap `max_pause_ms`, the catch-up time `max_catch_up_ms`, the resume countdown `resume_countdown_ms`, the log limit `max_log_bytes`). Demo rooms follow the server's setting, with a pause cap of at most 10 minutes (`kDemoMaxPauseMs`: the match of a link that nobody comes back to must not hold one of the few demo places for the server's 30; the control interface's rooms keep the server's cap), and when a Hello needs a demo place and none is free, **the demo room that has been abandoned the longest is ended and its place is given away** (every seat of a person held absent for at least a minute, `kDemoAbandonedMs`: a blip of every link at once cannot cost a match; its clients are dropped, its record is deleted, its log is freed, its end is reported; never a room with a person at it, a room that waits or one that loads; with no such room the answer is what it was: `NoSuchRoom`; `Room::abandoned_ms`, `RoomManager::evict_abandoned_public`; RJ1.19, RJ1.20; `/busy` is unchanged). A room without it is what it was: the Welcome's key is zero, a Hello for a running match is `MatchRunning`, a lost connection is a drop at once, nothing is logged, no Presence is sent.
* **The door.** `RoomManager::update` takes a Hello with a **non-zero key** for a room whose match runs and that holds seats to `Room::rejoin` (the session decides: `accept_rejoin`; a refused connection lingers for two seconds so that the Reject arrives, as any rejected Hello does). A key for a room that does not hold seats, that is loading, or a key that fits no seat is `MatchRunning`; a room that is over, or a demo room that ended, is `NoSuchRoom`, and **a Hello that shows a key never makes a room and never replaces an ended one** (the match of its key is gone: the player is told `NoSuchRoom` and its machine lets the key go; a room that is still being restored is waited for first: RJ1.6, RJ1.21); a key for a room that still waits is the lobby's (a machine that holds the key of its seat in a waiting room takes the seat back, same seat, key and place in the order of the Welcomes, so the leader stays the leader; the game keeps no key for a waiting room, see "The keys").
* **Connection ownership** (the use-after-free of the design, reproduced under AddressSanitizer): the room owns every connection (`connections_`, at most `RoomSpec::max_connections` = `kMaxConnections` = 32 that ever said Hello, closed unused ones pruned; **a returning connection beyond that is refused with `Reject Full`**, after a prune has freed what it can: the table never grows past its bound); the session holds raw pointers. **`prune_connections` asks `session_->uses_connection(c)`** before it frees a closed connection (the prototype's `PROTO_MUT=10`, without it: a heap-use-after-free in `pump_rejoiners` when a returning player's link closes in the pass in which the door reads another seat's Hello and the room keeps 32 connections: test_server S3.42, which runs in the ASan suite).
* **`Room::update`.** A held seat counts as present: a room whose players all lost their connection waits (each seat is held until the cap) and then ends "everybody left" (all seats dropped by the cap); `run_ms`, the wall-clock limit, counts the time that the match **ran** (`now - started - Attendance::held_ms`: the pauses as they really lasted and the countdowns after them), not the time that it waited. At the end of a match (finished or failed) the log is freed (`release_log`) and its size and the pause total are kept for the status.
* **The log's memory.** `max_log_bytes` per room (16 MiB by default; `--log-mb`) and a **budget for all the rooms together** (`ServerLimits::log_budget_bytes`, 256 MiB: `net::LogBudget`, shared by every `TurnLog`, **charged what a log has allocated, not what it stores**; an append whose growth the budget refuses stores nothing and **kills that log: it frees everything it held at once** and the room falls back to dropping a lost seat at once; what a log holds goes back when the match ends). `GET /stats` shows `log_bytes` and `log_budget_bytes`. **Limit (documented, not fixed): the budget is first come, first served.** A log that needs to grow while other logs hold the whole budget dies; sixteen hostile rooms that fill their logs are dead within seconds and give everything back (S3.56), and a room made afterwards logs again, but a room that was running meanwhile may have lost its way back: its status says so (`log.usable` false).
* **The status** (`GET /rooms/<code>`, the list, the result file `<code>.json`; every key is an addition, a lobby that ignores them works as before): `"reconnect"`, `"hold_vote_seconds"`, `"max_pause_seconds"`, `"max_catch_up_seconds"`, `"resume_countdown_seconds"` (the room's rules), `"paused"` (the match is held: a seat is missing, or the countdown after a pause runs), `"resume_seconds"` (the seconds left of that countdown, 0: none), `"absent": [{"seat", "name", "state": "absent|catching_up", "away_seconds", "progress"}]` (longest away first; running rooms only), `"vote": {"seat", "continue", "voters"} | null` (null when no vote is open **and when nobody can cast one**: a room whose only persons are the ones who are gone, the rest being bots, has an open vote with no voter, and that is shown as no vote), `"paused_seconds"` (the match's total pause so far, kept at the end), `"rejoins"`, `"drops_by_vote"`, `"drops_by_cap"` (the counters: they tell a seat dropped by the others' vote from one that left), `"rejoins_refused"` (Hellos with a key that a budget refused), `"catch_up_expired"` (absences whose catch-up time ran out), `"streamed_bytes"` (the log, streamed to returning players: at most three times its size per seat in ten minutes), `"log": {"turns", "bytes", "usable"}` (what the log held at the end of a finished match). **A seat's key is in none of it**, nor in a log line (the server logs a room's end with its pause, rejoins and drops, never a key; N: S3.46 searches the status, the list, the statistics, the result text and everything written to stderr for the keys' hex).
* **The control interface's room specification:** `"reconnect": true|false`, `"hold_vote_seconds"` (5 - 3600, default 30), `"max_pause_seconds"` (60 - 86400, default 1800), `"max_catch_up_seconds"` (10 - 3600, default 300), `"resume_countdown_seconds"` (0 - 60, default 10, 0 = none); what is left out is the server's default. **The server's options:** `--reconnect` / `--no-reconnect` (the last one wins), `--hold-vote-seconds N` (5 - 3600), `--max-pause-seconds N` (60 - 86400), `--max-catch-up-seconds N` (10 - 3600), `--resume-countdown-seconds N` (0 - 60), `--log-mb N` (1 - 256, the limit of one room's log); a value outside its range or a word that is no number stops the server with status 2 like the demo options (`tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` checks every edge), and the usage text names them. `RoomSpec::max_connections` (32) is not in the control interface: it is a bound of the server (a room cannot keep fewer than its players, `create_room` refuses).

### The flows

| flow | what happens |
|---|---|
| **a link is lost** | the host sees it (closed, a failed send, or 10 s of silence): the seat is Absent, nothing is sealed, a Presence goes to the others (`missing: [seat]`, `waited_s`, the cap's `cap_s`), once a second from then on |
| **it comes back in memory** (the page lives, the engine is intact) | new link, `Hello{key, have_turns}`, `Welcome{rejoin}`, `CatchUp{have_turns, total}`, the missing `TurnBatch` messages, the machine runs them at once, `CaughtUp{total, hash}`, the referee compares, `Presence` (the seat is back), sealing resumes at turn `total`: **about 0.3 s over links of 30 - 40 ms** (N2.47) |
| **it comes back from nothing** (a reloaded page, a game started again) | new link, `Hello{key, have_turns = 0}`, `Welcome{rejoin}`, `Start`, `Loaded`, `Begin`, `CatchUp{0, total}`, the whole stream (batches of at most 48 KB, a window of 256 KB), `CaughtUp`, the comparison, `Presence` |
| **somebody else is away too** | the match resumes only when nobody is Absent or CatchingUp; a seat that returns while another stays away is Present again and waits with the others |
| **the vote** | after 30 s away in all the others get `vote_seat` in their Presence and send `Vote{seat, continue}`; more than half of the connected players (2 of 2, 2 of 3, 3 of 4) drops the seat: its `Drop` is sealed in the first turn after the pause, the same tick on every machine; with two players the survivor wins |
| **the cap** | the match's total pause (any seat Absent or CatchingUp, each moment once, every pause at least 5 s) reaches `max_pause_ms`: **every seat that is not Present is dropped** (`drops_by_cap`), a catching-up seat that shows progress too; the others' match goes on after the countdown |
| **a second window with the key** | the host takes the seat over: the old window is sent `Reject Superseded` and closed and stops trying; the new one catches up |
| **the seat was dropped** | the key is answered `Reject Dropped` (the original's string 94, "Sorry, you have been dropped from the game"): the client stops |
| **the match goes on after a pause** | a pause of 3 s or more ends (a seat back and verified, a vote or the cap dropped the last missing seat): Presence `resume_s = 10`, once a second down to 0, nothing sealed, chat relayed, commands discarded; then the first turn (a loss during it cancels it, a pause under 3 s has none) |
| **a key holder that says Hello again and again** | the Hello of an attempt in progress changes nothing of it; the catch-up time of the absence (5 minutes, all attempts) ends the attempt; the 4th Hello of a minute, and a stream beyond three times the log in ten minutes, are `RejoinFailed`; the seat stays held: the vote and the cap apply (the review's grief took 176 s of pause with no drop; now the cap drops it at 60 s: measured below) |
| **a seat that flaps** | lost three times in a minute: the vote about it opens at once, whatever its state, the votes stay across its returns; the others drop it (its link is closed, told `Dropped`) or the minute passes without a loss |

### Lag is not a loss

| the client | a room that holds seats | a room that does not hold seats, and a LAN game |
|---|---|---|
| its link closed, a send to it failed | the seat is **held**, the match **pauses**, the way back is open | dropped at once (protocol 8) |
| nothing at all for 10 s (a hung window, a half-open link) | **held, the match pauses** | dropped after 60 s of silence in a LAN game; a server's room: a laggard (below) until 30 s without an acknowledgement |
| it talks (pings) but is slow, hidden or frozen for less than 10 s | **lag**: the others are told "Bob is lagging", nobody pauses, it catches up at up to 4x | the same |
| it talks but its acknowledgements do not move for 30 s, or it is 60 s behind | **dropped for good** (no hold, no pause) | the same |

### The native key travels in clear (a limit)

The key is a bearer secret: whoever knows it can take the seat. On the **WebSocket door** it travels inside TLS (behind the reverse proxy); on the **native TCP door** it travels in clear, like the room's `token` in the same Hello: a native client on a public server exposes it to whoever is on its path. The web page is the way for public games; a lobby that wants native clients on the internet should put them behind a VPN or a tunnel. A wrong key reveals nothing (`MatchRunning`, as for no key), a key is compared in constant time, one key lives as long as the match, and none is ever written to a log, a status or a result file.

### What the clients do in release B

**`NetGame` (built; `test_rejoin`, suite 2.19.1).** A client of a dedicated server's room that gives it a key (the session's `migration` is false: LAN and direct games are what they were, host migration stays theirs) builds its `ClientSession` with `reconnect`, `key`, `hello` (name, room, token, its seat) and `rejoin`; `update` makes the new links that `wants_connection` asks for (`TcpConnection` natively, `WasmWsConnection` in the browser, its `set_on_wake` as for the first link; a `JoinTarget` keeps how the first link was made; a link that cannot be made is `attach(nullptr)`: the next try is two seconds away, not a frame) and **`Phase` stays `Playing`** while the machine is away. **The reload path:** `join` / `join_url` take a `SeatKey`: the lobby's Hello shows it, Welcome with the rejoin flag, Start, the machine loads the map (the events `StartRequested` and `Begun` carry `rejoin`, so the screen skips the start dialog and the start sound) and its session starts in `CatchingUp`. A Welcome without the flag and with another key, or none, is a new player of the room that took the code (a waiting room that another player's Hello made: the server makes none for a Hello that shows a key): the old key is forgotten and the status line says "Your match has ended. This is a new room."; the same key is the seat taken back in a waiting room. **`BadRequest` to a Hello that carried turns** (the record lost its last second: "Writing") makes the NetGame start from nothing itself, with a new lobby and the same key, once per match; a second one ends it as a refusal does. **Keys leave through two callbacks:** `set_on_key` (a new player's key at the Start, a rejoin's at its Welcome: "The keys") and `set_on_forget_key` (the match ended, the seat was dropped, the match is over or the server does not hold the seat, the time ran out, the player left, a new room; both functions may end the session with `leave()`); `Superseded` (the other window has the seat) and `RejoinFailed` ("Try Rejoin in a minute") keep the key; no retry follows any refusal, and the words are `NetGame::way_back_text`; `leave()` after a session that ended by itself (Over, Failed) lets go of nothing more, because its end decided about the key (the application's way back to its menu calls it). **For the screens:** `pause_info()` (this machine's way back: reconnecting, seconds away, attempts, seconds before it gives up, catching up and its percent; the seats that are missing, with names and seconds; the vote and this machine's choice; the countdown), `paused()` (the server holds the match, or this machine does not follow it live: nothing is sealed, so a `Quit` command would be lost and the application calls `leave()`), `vote(keep_waiting)`, the event `Rejoined` (the way back is over). The prediction of one's own orders is off in every state of the way back and the gap of a page that was not woken reaches the session in every one of them. `NetGame::catching_up()` keeps its old meaning ("more than 3 s behind, running the backlog down": the HUD's "Catching up..."); the catch-up of a way back is `pause_info().catching_up`.

**Tests** (`test_rejoin`: real NetGames over loopback sockets against a real `RoomManager`, a stepped clock): RJ1.1 a cut link, RJ1.2 a restart over the records, RJ1.3 the reload path, RJ1.4 `BadRequest` (a record cut by 40 turns; the fallback is taken once), RJ1.5 every refusal (its words, its rule for the key, no retry), RJ1.6 a new room and a seat taken back, RJ1.7 the give-up, RJ1.8 the vote, RJ1.9 the prediction, RJ1.10 a room on the local network and one that holds no seats, RJ1.11 a link that cannot be made, RJ1.12 the gap of a page, RJ1.13 `leave()` in every state, RJ1.14 what the server is told (the Hello of the way back), RJ1.15 the key functions may end the session, RJ1.16 what the screens are told, field by field (a scripted server's Presence), RJ1.17 the server's budget of Hellos (the fourth rejoin of a minute: "Try Rejoin in a minute", and the Rejoin after the minute works), RJ1.18 a cut in the seconds of the start dialog, RJ1.19 - RJ1.24 the review's fixes (below). `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` (part reconnect) runs the real games: a stopped client comes back by itself, two games find a restored room.

**The application (built; `test_rejoin_store`, suite 3.23, and `test_rejoin_app`, suite 3.24).** What the player sees, top to bottom (`net_overlay.hpp` decides, `Application::render_net_overlay` draws; the first that applies wins, and nothing below it shows with it):

1. this machine's way back: "Connection lost. Reconnecting... 0:12" (the seconds since the loss, and " (attempt 3)" from the second link on) with "Esc leaves the match" under it;
2. the seats that are missing, after one second of pause (a blip is no banner): "Bob (Red) lost the connection, waiting 0:42" (a line a seat, three at the most; "Bob (Red) is coming back... 45%" for a seat that is given the match), and **the vote block** while a vote is open: "0 of 2 voted to continue" and two buttons, "F2 Keep waiting" and "F3 Continue without Bob" (the keys, or a press and a release on the button; the player's own choice is drawn pressed; "Bob (Red) keeps losing the connection" names a seat that is back and flaps). It is never a dialog: chat and the map work;
3. the countdown: "Bob is back: the match goes on in 7" ("The match goes on in 7" after a vote dropped the seat);
4. today's lines: the desync (red), the election, "Waiting for the other players...", "Catching up...", lag, notices.

A name is cut with "..." to fit the view (442 px in the original's picture, 762 px in the 16:9 one); every text is measured at both shapes.

**The catch-up screen** (decision 5): while `pause_info().catching_up`, and from the BadRequest fallback's refusal until it has caught up, the screen is the loading screen's picture with the bar filled to the percent, "Catching up 45%" on the strip and "Esc leaves the match" at its right end (`draw_catch_up_screen`); no key, click, typed text or wheel reaches the match meanwhile, the FPS and version corner stays. The engine's sounds and status news of the replayed turns are dropped at the first live tick (the replay of a whole match would play them at once; the chat log's News Flash lines stay). **A rejoin does not start a match:** `Begun` / `StartRequested` with `rejoin` give no "Get ready" dialog, no start sound and no start news (`HUD::init(.., announce)`), and no replay of the waiting room's talk; a second begin of one session (the BadRequest fallback) re-enters the match cleanly. A way back that ends with `Rejoined` resets nothing of the HUD (selection, view, chat log, pedestals). **The start dialog yields:** it is closed when the match is paused (it would swallow the keys and hide the overlay) and does not come back. **Leaving:** while `paused()` (a seat is missing, the countdown runs, or this machine is on its way back), and while the catch-up screen is up in the lobby phases of a match that starts again after a BadRequest (no session plays there: `paused()` is false and the command would go nowhere), the quit dialog's Yes calls `NetGame::leave()` and goes back as a quit does (a `Quit` command would be discarded: no turn is sealed; RA6.1, RA6.4); Esc on the way back opens that dialog (a held Esc asks once; elsewhere Esc is the original's: deselect).

**The keys** (`rejoin_store.hpp`; the key is a bearer secret: it is in no URL, log line, status text or test output). `set_on_key` writes it, `set_on_forget_key` removes it, for every room of a server. **When:** a new player's key is announced when the **Start** arrives (the match begins: a waiting room that is only visited, or a tab that is closed in it, leaves no key behind to be offered for a match that never began), a rejoin's at its Welcome, and a start that is **cancelled** takes the key back (the next Start gives it again; RJ1.6, RJ1.22). A reload in a waiting room is a new visit: it asks for the seat that its address names (below) and gets it when it is free. Desktop: the file `rejoin.txt` next to the settings, mode 0600 on POSIX (opened with 0600 and `fchmod`ed, the temp file of the atomic write too; the per-user folder is private on Windows), a line a key, tab-separated: server, room, seat, key (32 hex digits), written (Unix seconds); at most 8 (the newest), none older than **3 hours** (`kRejoinMaxAgeMs`: a match of the site's server is over well within it; dropped when read or written), a broken line ignored, written to a temp file and renamed over the old one, forgotten only by the exact server, room, seat **and key**. Web: `localStorage` `ants.rejoin.<room>.<seat>` = `{"k": hex, "s": server, "t": epoch ms}` (3 hours, removed when read; `REJOIN_MAX_AGE_MS`, the same number in `lobby.html` and `shell.html`). **Which key a join uses:** a join that names a room (`--join ADDR --room CODE [--seat N]`, `--join-url`, the web page's join arguments) finds the newest fresh entry of that server and room (of the seat, with `--seat`) and joins with it, asking for the entry's seat; a join without a room, another room, another server or an old entry uses none. The start menu's first panel offers **"Rejoin your match (CODE)"** above Single player while a fresh key of a `host:port` server is in the file (the newest; it joins that server, room and seat with that key; a refusal comes back to the first panel with the reason and lets go of the key when the rule says so).

**Tests** (`test_rejoin_app`, a real room manager over loopback with bare second machines on a stepped clock): RA1 the way back (the overlay's words and seconds; the catch-up screen with its percent, drawn and blocking; the HUD as it was; the key file; what the replay queued), RA2 the missing seat and the vote (keys, mouse, a dialog over the match), RA3 the countdown, RA4 a game started again (which key a join uses; the start without the start), RA5 the dialog that gives way, RA6 quit and Esc while held, RA7 the start menu's Rejoin, RA8 every state against the one line shown and every text at both shapes, RA9 the second begin of the BadRequest fallback, RA6.3 / RA6.4 leaving on purpose and quitting in the lobby phases, RA10 the page is told the seat.

### The switch, the front page's Rejoin button and the check in a real browser (work package 4)

**The switch is on.** `kReconnectByDefault` (`room_manager.hpp`) is `true`: the server's rooms, the demo rooms and the control interface's rooms hold seats and keep restart records unless the server is started with `--no-reconnect` or the room says `"reconnect": false` ("The switch", above; a `RoomSpec` that code builds still says `false` unless it asks). Rewritten for the new default, none deleted: `test_server` S3.47 (and S3.97, now "The Switch Is On": the constant, `ServerLimits`, a manager's, a control interface's and a demo room, a `--no-reconnect` server, a restored room), `test_start_menu_app` A6.2 and A13.3 (both settings), and the demo part of `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` (the stack's options have no reconnect option; the log line; a `--no-reconnect` server). Network protocol 12, the simulation and every hash are unchanged.

**The front page's Rejoin button** (`web/lobby.html`, blocks `REJOINKEY` and `REJOIN`: pure functions, run by node in `tests/scripts/web_rejoin_block_check.js`; the page as a whole in `web_name_check.js`; the file's pins in `test_web_rejoin.py`). While this browser holds a fresh key of this site's game server, a strip sits at the top of the page (below the notice banner, above the header; a block that is hidden takes no room) with the sentence **"Your match in room CODE is still running: go back to your seat."** and ONE button, **"Rejoin it"**. An entry counts when its name is `ants.rejoin.<room>.<seat>` (the page's own room-code rule, seat 0 - 3), its value is exactly the game's JSON (`k` 32 hex digits, not all zero; `s` the server; `t` the time in ms), `s` is the address that the game page would join from this origin (`ws://host/ws` on http, `wss://host/ws` on https: the same rule as `joinArguments`), and `t` is not older than 3 hours or more than a minute ahead; the newest is offered. An entry older than three hours is removed when read, as the game does; another server's, one from the future and anything that is no entry are left alone. Pressing the button takes THIS tab to `?join=/ws&room=CODE&seat=N&name=<the remembered name>&aspect=<the remembered shape>`: the key is in no address, text or log (the game page finds it in the storage). The block is looked at when the page runs the lobby (every address of the page: a plain visit or a link to a room), again when another tab writes to the storage, and a page that comes back from the browser's memory (Back) is loaded again, so it looks again by itself. The lobby's START uses the same entry the other way round: when the leader's START is heard, the page writes `ants.rejoin.<room>.<seat>` = `{"k", "s", "t"}` (`rejoinEntry` of the client) to the storage, forgets the room that its tab kept and goes to that same game-page address (the plain code, the seat, the name, the shape); the game page takes the seat over with the key. The lobby never calls `leave()` at START (it sends no Leave and does not close its socket), and the strip stays hidden from then on.

**The game page does not ask a key holder for a name** (found by the browser check: the plan said that the game page needed no change). The name step asks whenever the address names no player, and the page drops `name=` from the address on purpose (a copy of the address then asks its owner). A game that was reloaded in the middle of a match was therefore asked for a name and never rejoined. A browser that holds the key of this room's seat on this door is not asked (`holdsThisSeat` in the name gate); a shared link for another seat or room, another server's key, an old or a broken entry and a storage that cannot be read ask as before. The keys block `REJOINKEY` is one text in `lobby.html` and `shell.html` (a test asserts that they are equal).

**The check in a real browser** (`tests/scripts/web_rejoin_check.py`, opt-in; `tests/scripts/test_web_rejoin.sh` is its wrapper: it skips unless `ANTS_WEB_URL` and `ANTS_WS_PORT` are set, and the check ends with 3 when the environment is not there). It needs the web image of the tree behind a site whose `/ws` leads to a port, a Chromium-based browser and the `ants_server` of the tree, which the check starts itself as the stack does (`docker-compose.stack.yml`'s demo options, no reconnect option), with a results folder, on the WebSocket port that the site's `/ws` leads to (`--ws-port`) and free ports for the rest. Each player is a browser with a profile and a storage of its own.
