The game's words are canvas pixels, so the check reads them through `ants_probe(16)` (the number of lines of the net overlay) and `ants_probe(10000 + 1000 * line + index)` (the byte at that place, 0 past the end), a read-only addition to the probe that was there (`net_overlay_probe`, `test_hud_layout`); the probes wait for the page's `isReadyToPlay` (index.js binds each export to the program at its first call, and a call during the compile leaves it undefined for the life of the page; the page's own timer that wakes a hidden game, `wake` in `web/shell.html`, had the same fault, found with the probes: a page that loaded hidden over a slow connection lost its pump and its timer for good, so `wake` waits for `isReadyToPlay` too). A key is looked for in every address, page text and console line of the browsers and is never printed. Run against the image of this tree, the fixes of the review included (below), 87 checks, 0 failed, 207 s, and twice more with the same result (193 s and 193 s):

| Part | What it does | What it showed |
|---|---|---|
| `reload` | two players in a room for two (the first one's link is a shared one with no seat in it); the first reloads its page: once quickly (everything in the browser's cache), once slowly (every request 1.5 s late) | quick: back in the running match in 1.0 s, no "Get ready" dialog, a pause of 0.7 s is a blip (under 3 s: no banner, no countdown, the other screen says nothing). Slow: back in 6.8 s, the seat was away for 14.9 s; the other screen said "Ann (Green) lost the connection, waiting 0:06", then "Ann is back: the match goes on in 10", then nothing. The state hashes of the two games (every 100 ticks) were equal after each. The address of each page carried the seat that the room gave it, once (`seat=0`, `seat=1`: the game told its page), also after both reloads |
| `restart` | the server gets SIGTERM and starts again over the same folder | the server stops in 0.00 s with its record made durable; both pages say "Connection lost. Reconnecting... 0:00" and "Esc leaves the match", come back by themselves 8 and 9 s after the stop (the server was started 7.3 s after it), the room says that it came back from its record, the hashes agree. The pause is counted from the restore, so players who are back within 3 s of it resume at once, with no countdown |
| `rejoin` | the first player's tab is closed; a new tab opens the front page | the other screen says "Ann (Green) lost the connection, waiting 0:05"; the key is still in the storage; the button and its line are there with the words above, above the cards; the press goes to the game page in the same tab, with no key in the address; back in the running match in 1.0 s, the other screen says "Ann is back: the match goes on in 10"; the hashes agree |
| `leave` | two players in a match; the second leaves through the page's Menu button and answers the question | the page asks "Leave the game and go back to the menu?" and goes to the front page; the server never paused the room for that seat (it was dropped at once: a closed tab would have held it and stopped the match for the first player), the first player's screen never said that a connection was lost (only "Waiting for the other players..."), the key was gone from the second browser's storage and the front page offered no Rejoin |
| `none` | the entries that must not be offered | no entry: nothing is shown and nothing moves; older than three hours: not offered and removed; another server's (or the other scheme of this host): not offered, kept; six kinds of malformed entry: not offered, not removed; the newest of two; the address with the remembered name and shape; at 390 px with a room code of 32 characters the page does not scroll sideways |

**Known limits** (found by the work; none is a rule of the game). (1) A fast reload is a blip: the seat is away for under 3 s, so there is no countdown and the other player sees nothing. (2) A key is offered for as long as the browser holds it (3 hours at the most) although the match may be over: the page cannot ask the server (only `/busy`, a count, is public). For a room that no longer exists, or a demo room that ended, the server answers `NoSuchRoom` to a Hello that carries a key and makes no room for it: the game forgets the key and says "The match is over." (before the review's M3 a demo room was made again by its code, and the person sat alone in a new room of that name). (3) A restart that comes back within 3 s of the restore resumes without a countdown; a player who comes back later gets it (`rejoin` and the slow `reload` show that path). (4) The check runs Chromium only, so `BrowserStorage` (the browser's part of the key store, built by CI before this) has been run in one browser and not in Firefox or Safari. (5) Two windows of one machine race for the key file (the last writer wins; the server's `Superseded` rule decides which window plays), and the web storage has one entry per room and seat (a second server with the same room code and seat replaces it); the address of a web game carries the seat that the room gave its window (L2, below), so a reload takes its own seat and key when two windows of one browser play one room. (6) **A native player's key travels in clear** on the public stack: the native TCP door has no TLS (as the room's token), the web door is inside TLS, so a person on the path of a native player's connection can read the key and take the seat. An operator who does not accept that has two options: `--no-reconnect` (no keys, no held seats) or no published native port (take the `4001` line out of the `ports` of `docker-compose.stack.yml`, or close the port: the web page and its `/ws`, which is inside TLS, need none). (7) **The Hello budget ends the automatic way back**: the fourth Hello of a minute is refused with `RejoinFailed`, the way back ends with "Try Rejoin in a minute" and keeps the key (the front page's or the start menu's Rejoin button takes the player back after the minute: RJ1.17); a later item: one automatic retry after 60 s.

### The limits

| what | number |
|---|---|
| lost = link closed, a send failed, or silence | 10 s (`silence_ms`) |
| the vote opens at | 30 s away in all (`vote_after_ms`; a room's `hold_vote_seconds` 5 - 3600), each loss at least 5 s (`min_absence_ms`) |
| a catch-up that makes no progress (no higher percent) is let go after | 20 s (`catch_up_stall_ms`) |
| the match's total paused time | 30 minutes (`max_pause_ms`, a room's `max_pause_seconds` 60 - 86,400), every pause counts at least 5 s; **absolute**: at the cap every seat that is not Present is dropped |
| a seat's catch-up time in one absence | 5 minutes over all its attempts (`max_catch_up_ms`, a room's `max_catch_up_seconds` 10 - 3600); then the catch-up fails and a Hello is refused |
| a seat's Hellos | 12 accepted a minute (`rejoin_attempts`, `rejoin_window_ms`); the 13th is `RejoinFailed` (a refused one costs nothing) |
| the log streamed to one seat | 3 times its size in 10 minutes, at least 1 MiB (`stream_factor`, `stream_window_ms`, `stream_floor_bytes`) |
| flapping | 3 losses within 60 s (`flap_losses`, `flap_window_ms`): the vote opens at once, in any state, and closes after 60 s without a loss |
| the resume countdown | 10 s after a pause of 3 s or more (`resume_countdown_ms`, a room's `resume_countdown_seconds` 0 - 60; the library's default is none): no turn is sealed, Presence tells the seconds once a second; it is not a pause (not the cap's, not a catch-up's) |
| Presence | on every change, and once a second while the match is held (a pause, the countdown after it) |
| the stream of the log | a window of 256 KB in bytes (`stream_window_bytes`) beyond what the machine has executed, batches of at most 4096 turns and 48 KB |
| the turn log | 16 MiB per room (`--log-mb` 1 - 256), 256 MiB for all rooms together (`ServerLimits::log_budget_bytes`), 36,000 turns = 30 minutes; at most `0xFFFFFFF0` bytes; freed when the match ends |
| a TurnBatch | at most 4096 turns and 48 KB (after the first turn); a message is at most 64 KB; a turn that does not fit a message makes the log unusable |
| a key | 16 bytes (128 random bits from the operating system) |
| connections of a room | at most 32 that ever said Hello (`RoomSpec::max_connections`; closed unused ones are pruned, the session's are never freed under it); a returning connection when all 32 are in use is told `Full` and refused |
| a client's attempts | one at once, then every 2 s (`reconnect_every_ms`); no Welcome in 10 s abandons one; it gives up after **the cap that the last Presence named and a minute** (31 minutes before any Presence: `reconnect_give_up_ms`) counted from the first loss, unless a Reject comes first |

### Measured (release A, native, Release build, Apple silicon, a machine that was shared)

* **A link cut, a machine that comes back in memory** (`test_lockstep` N2.47, links of 30 - 40 ms one way): the host pauses **20 ms** after the cut (one pass of 10 ms steps), the others know **60 ms** after it, and the match resumes **140 ms** after the new link was made (Hello, Welcome, `CatchUp`, the turns it lacks, `CaughtUp`, the comparison, `Presence`: about 0.3 s from the cut to the first new turn when the machine asks at once).
* **A machine that starts from nothing** (N2.48): 801 turns (11 KB of log) were given and verified **290 ms** after the page opened (the lobby's four trips and the stream over links of 30 - 40 ms).
* **The work of a catch-up** (the real `HostSession`, `ClientLobby` and `ClientSession` over a link of 1 ms, so that what is measured is work; four players, each giving an order every 400 ms: about 10 orders a second, a busy match; a scratch program, not part of the suite): **5 minutes of play: 6,001 turns, 103,078 bytes of log, 1.27 - 1.39 s; 15 minutes: 18,001 turns, 309,566 bytes, 3.72 - 3.94 s; 30 minutes: 36,001 turns, 618,906 bytes, 7.26 - 7.48 s** (two runs, on a machine that was busy with other builds), all of it the machine's own `fast_forward` (the host's share, the stream and the comparison, was 0.3 - 0.75 ms), the state equal to the referee's at the end. The cost is the simulation itself (about 4,800 ticks a second in this world), not the network; the original maps are 6 - 12 minutes long.
* **A hostile log** (N2.63): two players that put 40 commands of 32 ants into every turn made a log of **5.4 MB in 503 turns**; it was streamed to a machine that started from nothing over a link that fails at 1 MB of backlog: 5,435,622 bytes sent, **at most 305 KB in flight** at any moment, the link never failed.
* **The real server** (`tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh`: the real `ants_server --reconnect --max-pause-seconds 60` and headless game clients, a client behind `flaky_proxy.py` that is cut, a client that is stopped): **the room paused 0.1 s after the link was cut** (the proxy closes both ends, so the server sees the close at once), named the absent seat, did not advance while it waited (ticks equal six seconds apart), **and the cap dropped the seat 60.0 s after the cut** (60 s of pause) with the counters in the status and the result file and the match going on at 20 ticks a second; a client stopped with `kill -STOP`: **the room paused 10.1 s later** (10 s of silence is a loss, 8 s is not) with the seat named absent, not lagging; the client that woke up found its link closed and ended with the lost-connection message; 125 checks in all (43 of them new), no key in the status, the result file or the log.
* **The review of release A, measured again after the fixes** (the review's drivers, built against the fixed code; the real-server ones against the `ants-server` image, `docker create` + `start`; before = the review's own numbers): **a Hello every 12 s** (`exp_grief`, a cap of 60 s): the room was held **176 s** with no drop (a catch-up that was restarted by every Hello); now the pause ended at the cap, **60 s**, `drops_by_cap` 1, the vote open from 54 s, two Hellos refused by a budget (the server's log says "2 refused by a budget"); the anonymous demo room of `exp_demo_grief` the same (the cap dropped the seat at 60 s). **A log of 14.1 MB and a key holder that says Hello again and again** (`exp_bigstream2`): 50 full streams in 10 s (69 MB/s) before; now **three streams (42 MB, three times the log) and then 3,764 Hellos refused in 10 s**, each costing 46 bytes in and a few out. **Jitter buffers after a short pause** (`drv_jit`, three cuts, links of 5 and of 20 ms, pauses of 40 and 100 ms): the others' buffers **1, 1, 1** and 12 s later 1 (they were 2, then 4). **A connection cut every 300 ms** (`drv_flapspeed`, 40 s, 133 cuts): paused **1 %** of the time (580 ms) at **99 %** speed, the seat voted out (72 % paused at 34 % speed before). **A hostile room that fills its log** (`exp_hostile_log3`, the real image, next to a good room): the budget's use fell from 26,355,712 to **16,384** bytes the moment the hostile log died at the room's limit of 16 MiB, the process's memory from 33.2 to 16.5 MB, and the good room's log was never touched. **Chaos** (`drv_chaos`, random cuts, reloads, freezes, votes and leaves on a simulated network, 240 s of virtual time each, from clocks near the wrap): **320 seeds, 0 failed checks**, every machine and the referee identical at the end. **The 30-minute soak** (`drv_soak`, three seeds, about 6 - 14 s between cuts, a page reloaded every 15 - 45 s, a machine frozen for 12 s every 150 s; the budgets of a key holder raised so that the chaos can run): **3 seeds of 30 minutes each** (172 - 177 cuts, 55 - 60 reloads, 11 freezes and 232 - 240 returns a seed, a log that grew to 552 KB, the memory of the process that held it moving by 8.4 - 9.6 MB over the half hour): no machine was lost, **every machine and the referee ended in the same state, no desync** (the soak of the review cut a seat about every 3.5 s, which the budget of three Hellos a minute refuses by design: such a client ends with `RejoinFailed`, and the soak's chaos is spread so that no seat is cut more than a few times a minute; the budgets of the key holders are raised there).
* **Checked again on the release tree (v0.0.98: the work rebased onto v0.0.97), with the `ants-server` and `ants-beta` images on a private docker network** (the web page, the server with demo rooms and no `--reconnect`: the way the owner's stack runs it; headless Chrome with a throwaway profile, native clients, and raw clients that speak protocol 10). **Default (`reconnect` off, as in v0.0.97):** a web page seat and a native client played a demo room at 19.99 ticks a second; two native clients at 19.97; a native seat whose link was cut was dropped at once (the two-player match ended 0.11 - 0.12 s after the cut, the status never showed `paused` or `absent`, `drops_by_cap` and `rejoins` 0); the web page's own check (`tests/scripts/test_web_hidden.sh`, a hidden tab for 45 s) ran at 20.00 ticks a second with equal state hashes and no Lag notice; a Hello of protocol 9 is answered `VersionMismatch`. **A room made with `"reconnect": true` through the control interface** (two raw clients and a native client behind `flaky_proxy.py`): the Welcome carries a key per seat; the cut paused the room within the 50 ms of the poll, the status named the seat (`absent`), nothing ran while it waited (143 -> 144 ticks in 3 s), the vote opened 5.1 s after the cut (`hold_vote_seconds` 5: `{seat 2, continue 0, voters 2}`), one vote of two was not enough (the match stayed paused), the second dropped the seat (`drops_by_vote` 1) and the status then counted the resume countdown 10, 9, ... 1 (`resume_seconds`; Presence carried the same `resume_s` to a raw client, with nobody missing), after which the match ran again at 19.98 ticks a second (`paused_seconds` 6: the countdown is no pause); with a cap of 60 s and nobody voting the cap dropped the seat **60.1 s** after the cut (`paused_seconds` 60), a 10 s countdown followed and the match went on at 20.15 ticks a second; neither the status JSON nor the container's log held a key. **The leader's START** (a room for four, two raw clients, with and without `reconnect`): a guest's StartRequest is ignored, the leader's starts the match for both (Start and Begin seen) at 19.96 / 19.95 ticks a second; and `test_start_menu_app --real-server` (the start menu's host, join and START against the image) reports `REAL SERVER OK`.

### Tests

Part 1 (the building blocks): `test_lockstep` **N2.40** (every message of protocol 10 byte by byte, every truncation and trailing byte, every range rule, the batch encoders agree, the old layouts of 6 - 9 are refused but their version is read), N2.1 - N2.3 (updated: the round trips, the type ranges, the 400,000-message fuzz with the five new decoders), **N2.41** (36,000 real-looking turns, batches of every size, the limit to the byte), **N2.42 - N2.44** (the attendance table, the totals, the vote rule for 1 - 3 connected with every number of votes, the cap, the stall, the wrap, Presence), **N2.45** (fast_forward against the paced runner), **N2.46** (the jitter buffer and the catch-up); `test_jitter` J1.11 (`reset`); `test_lobby` **N4.14** (keys), **N4.15** (the takeover), **N4.16** (the rejoin flow through `ClientLobby`), N4.2c (updated: the old layouts); `test_netgame` N3.17 (the texts of the new rejections); `test_server` S3.3 (extended: raw Hellos of 6 - 9 at the door), **S3.58** (`random_bytes`).

Part 2 (the sessions and the server), in `test_lockstep` on the simulated network (the rig `HoldMatch`: a seatless host that holds seats, the keys, a door that takes a Hello with a key to `accept_rejoin`, the machines' own reconnect, a machine that is reloaded with the real `ClientLobby`; and `LoneSession`: one `ClientSession` against a scripted server): **N2.47** a lost link pauses within one pass, nothing is sealed for 20 s, the others are told, the held seat gates nothing, the in-memory return resumes at the right schedule, three machines identical; **N2.48** a machine from nothing is given more than 300 turns (and one with a slowed catch-up that lasts past 30 s of away time is never put to the vote and never dropped while it progresses); **N2.49** the vote (not before 30 s, one of two is not enough, a changed choice counts, the `Drop` at the same tick everywhere, the key told "dropped"); **N2.50** the cap (every seat that is not present is dropped, a catching-up seat that progresses too; a stalled one is let go and then dropped); **N2.51** two seats gone (the longer away first, the next vote starts empty); **N2.52** wrong key, no key, a made-up count of turns, a second window that takes a live seat over (`Superseded`), a newer attempt closes the older, a session with no Start message refuses a machine that has nothing (`RejoinFailed`) and still takes back one that has its turns; **N2.53** a different state after the catch-up (a `Desync` to that machine only, the room does not fail); **N2.54** a full log falls back to dropping at once (and refuses a second window with a live seat's key, `RejoinFailed`, without replacing the window that plays), the log is appended before the send, a seat without a key cannot be held; **N2.55** 400 commands in a pause are no offence, a malformed one is; **N2.56** `Leave` is final; **N2.57** absences add up, each loss counts 5 s; **N2.58** two reloads at once; **N2.59** a stalled catch-up is let go and the second try gets through from the turns it has; **N2.60** Presence per recipient; **N2.61** chat during a pause; **N2.62** a bot's seat; **N2.63** the hostile log; **N2.64** a Reject ends the session in every mode, whatever the reason; **N2.65** the way back (the Hello when the link opens, the attempts every 2 s, the give-up, an attempt without a Welcome, leaving, finishing); **N2.66** the catch-up through a scripted server, every protocol failure, the gating of `submit`, `chat` and `vote`; **N2.67** lag is not a loss (a window that stops for 5 s and for 9 s, acknowledgements that never arrive, 60 s behind); **N2.68** silence is a loss after 10 s (a hung window, a half-open link); **N2.69** a host that holds no seats is exactly what it was; **N2.70** the referee's runner and a seat lost in the first 50 ms; **N2.71** a returning connection is held to the rules of a client, the stream is paced in bytes; **N2.72** the same timeline from five different clocks (8 s before the wrap, at the signed half, across the wrap); **N2.73** the log's budget; **N2.74** a silent server is silent in real time and only when it was asked (`note_gap`: the gap counts against a server that was asked and did not answer, one that was not asked yet gets it only up to 3 s short of the 10 s, 2.9 s after the question no and 3.1 s yes, a live server that answers its pings or has data waiting is never condemned, and the way back counts its waits in real time: the catch-up, a Hello without a Welcome, the attempts, the give-up). In `test_server`, through the real manager, door, lobby and room: **S3.59** keys, **S3.36** pause and return and a whole TINY match to its end identical on three machines, **S3.37** a reload of more than 700 turns, **S3.38** the vote ends a two-player match with the survivor the winner, **S3.39** `run_ms` counts play, **S3.40** everybody lost, **S3.41** the cap through the door, **S3.42** the prune window (**it runs in the AddressSanitizer suite**: without the fix it is a heap-use-after-free), **S3.43** connections that are cut 40 times (three players take turns: a seat is taken back three times a minute), **S3.44** the door and a key (wrong, none, over, loading, not holding seats), **S3.45** a reload in the waiting room, **S3.46** the status JSON and no key anywhere, **S3.47** the settings and their bounds, **S3.48** the budget for the logs, **S3.49** a slow machine is lag, **S3.50** a room that does not hold seats is what it was (the scenarios of S3.7 and S3.12, said out loud), **S3.51** silence. `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh`: the options and their bounds, and a real server with a proxy that is cut and a client that is stopped.

**The review of release A** (an independent review built drivers, mutated the code and ran a real server; its findings are fixed with tests that fail without the fix, and its drivers were run again, below). New tests, in `test_lockstep`: **N2.75** the catch-up budget (a second Hello of an attempt changes nothing of it, the attempts of one absence add up, the budget fails a catch-up that shows progress at that millisecond, refuses a Hello, leaves the vote and the cap to work; caught up and a new loss give it back), **N2.76** the rejoin budget (three Hellos a minute to the millisecond, three times the log in ten minutes with and without the floor, a refused Hello changes nothing and costs nothing, the same from four clocks), **N2.77** flapping (three losses in a minute, the vote at once in every state, the votes across the returns, the closing minute, a second window counts, the 5 s of every pause reach the cap), **N2.78** the resume countdown (3 s to the millisecond, the seconds, a loss cancels it and one follows, a vote or the cap or a `Leave` begins it, not the cap's time), **N2.79** the runner is held (pauses of 40 ms to 30 s, no stall, the buffer stays, a rough link keeps what it learned), **N2.80** a key holder cannot freeze a room (a Hello every 12 s with progress), **N2.81** a key holder cannot stream the log again and again (100 Hellos in 10 s, the bytes alone), **N2.82** the others' jitter buffers after short pauses (the review's `drv_jit`), **N2.83** a connection that flaps (voted out while it is back, or refused at its fourth Hello), **N2.84** the countdown through whole matches (after a return, a blip, a loss during it, off, after a vote, after the cap, not a catch-up's time), **N2.85** a dead log frees its memory, **N2.86** the client's give-up follows the cap, **N2.87** Presence at once for a second loss and once a second and no faster; in `test_server` through the real manager: **S3.52** the countdown (status, ticks, the wall-clock limit), **S3.53** the new settings and their bounds, **S3.54** the grief through the door, **S3.55** the amplification through the door (the counters, no key), **S3.56** sixteen hostile rooms (their logs are dead and the budget is whole, a room made afterwards holds and returns a seat), **S3.57** a room keeps at most its bound of connections (`Full`). `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh`: the two new options and their edges, the countdown after the cap against a real server.

**Rewritten tests** (they pinned what the review showed to be wrong; each was changed to the corrected rule, none deleted or disabled): **N2.40** (the Presence layout has `resume_s` and a vote may be about any seat), **N2.41** and **N2.73** (a log that is dead holds nothing; the budget counts capacity), **N2.54** (the same, through the session), **N2.42** (every pause counts at least 5 s toward the cap; "a second try: the percent starts again" became "a Hello of an attempt that goes on changes nothing of it"), **N2.43** and **N2.71** (a seat is taken back three times a minute: the rules are raised in the blocks that say Hello many times), **N2.44** and **N2.50** ("a seat that progresses stays CatchingUp past 60 s" became "the cap is absolute: it is dropped at the cap"), **N2.47** (the others' runners are held, not stalled), **N2.57** (a connection that is lost three times in a minute is put to the vote at its third loss, not its seventh), `test_server` **S3.42** and **S3.43** (the cuts are spread over the seats and over time: a seat is taken back three times a minute), **S3.48** (a dead log gives its memory back, so the budget is no longer "full for ever"); the rigs: `HoldMatch` starts a reloaded session with its own clock (`clock_of`), and `held_spec` has the countdown off (every other test would wait ten seconds after every return).

## Restart records: a running match survives a restart of the server

Request of the owner: that the server must not "keep rebooting and kicking them out of the game" (the people who play online), and so a running match has to survive a restart of the server. Before this section every redeploy (it recreates the server's container) and every crash ended every running match: the server held each match only in memory. Now a room that holds seats (protocol 10, "Reconnect") keeps a **restart record** on the results volume from the moment its match is fixed until the room is over, and a server that starts again brings the matches back, with every seat held, so that the players come back with their keys. **No message and no protocol number changed** (the version stays 12): the way back is the one of "Reconnect"; what is new is that the server survives it.

**What a player sees.** The server's rooms keep records (every room holds seats unless the server is started with `--no-reconnect` or the room says `"reconnect": false`), and after a restart each of them is back with its code, paused, every seat held, the others able to vote after 90 s and the cap of the pauses (30 minutes by default) in force. The game's `NetGame` finds the room again by itself (it tries every two seconds, `test_rejoin` RJ1.2) and is given the match: a restart is some seconds of "Connection lost. Reconnecting...", then the catch-up screen, then the match goes on where it was ("What the clients do in release B"). With `--no-reconnect` nothing is kept (a room that holds no seats cannot hold them across a restart).

### The record (`restart_record.hpp`, `restart_record.cpp`)

One file per room in `<results-dir>/restart/` (or `--restart-dir`): `room-<code>-<8 hex digits of FNV-1a of the code>.restart` (the digits keep two codes that differ in case apart on a file system that ignores case; the prefix keeps a room named like a Windows device from naming one). Little endian throughout.

| | |
|---|---|
| file | the magic `ANTSRST1` (the digit is the format: a file of another format is refused as such), then frames |
| frame | `u8 type, u32 length (at most 1 MiB), payload, u32 CRC-32` of the type, the length and the payload |
| HEAD (1) | the first frame, once: the identity of the build that wrote it (game version, protocol, build id: **only the protocol decides**, the others are for the log), the room's specification (code, map and the map file's hash, players, fog, early start, the times, the reconnect settings, the limit of the turn log), the bots (the specification's and the leader's fill, which are told apart), the **start message** of the match (`net::StartMsg` in its wire form, so that the protocol's strict decoder reads it back: seed, roster, names, fog; **the clients' addresses are cleared**: a record has no business keeping them) and the **keys** of the four seats |
| TURNS (2) | `u32 first_turn, u16 count (1 - 4096)`, then `count` turns, each packed as the server's turn log packs it (`u16 command count`, the commands in their wire form): numbered from 0 without a hole; the server writes one turn to a frame |
| CHECK (3) | `u32 turn, u64 hash`: the referee's state hash (`StateHash::total`) after turn `turn`, written when the referee's own runner has run it: every 20th turn (the runner already takes it for the clients' comparison) |

The keys are secrets (a key is all that a seat's owner needs to take it back): the file is for its owner only (POSIX: mode 600 in a folder of mode 700; on Windows it takes the permissions of its folder, like the control secret's file), and a key is in **no log line, status, JSON or result file** (the test collects every text that the server makes through a restart and looks for the keys' hex: S3.91, and the real program's log in S3.95 / S3.96). Nothing of the engine's state is stored: the engine is rebuilt from the start message and the turns, which are the match.

### Writing: what is written when, and why

* **When.** A record is made when the room's start is accepted (the start message and the keys are known: `Loading`, so the seconds of the map's loading and the 5 s of the start dialog before the first turn are covered), **before the Start message is sent to anybody** (`HostLobby::set_before_start`: no machine can act on a Start whose match has no record; S3.108), is written for as long as the match runs and is **deleted** when the room finishes or fails (the owner's `DELETE /rooms/<code>` included) or when the start is cancelled (a machine that could not load the map). A waiting room has none. A room that holds no seats, or a server that keeps no records, has none either and its status says why.
* **The head is made all at once**: written to a temporary file next to the record (made new, mode 600), fsync'ed, given its name by a `rename` that replaces what was there, and the folder fsync'ed (best effort: some file systems refuse a sync of a folder, and the rename is then as durable as that file system makes it): a crash leaves no record or a whole head, never half of one (test S3.82 kills a process at its first write).
* **Every sealed turn is `write()`n to the record before it is sent to anybody** (`HostSession::set_on_seal`: after the turn is in the turn log, before the broadcast and before the referee's own runner has it). One `write()` call per turn, nothing buffered. **Why this order:** the operating system keeps what a process has written even if the process dies at that instant (a crash, `kill -9`, the container's death; the volume is a directory of the host), so *the record always holds every turn that any player has run*, and a restart **loses no turn that a player has seen** (S3.88 checks it at every 10 ms step of 30 s of play; S3.96 kills the real program in the middle of a match: the record held 154 turns and the machines had been sent 154; S3.95's SIGTERM runs found 154 or 155 in the record against 154 sent: the record may hold one turn more than any machine has seen, never fewer). A turn that was sealed and not yet written was never sent, so the restarted server seals it again, perhaps with other commands, and nobody has run the old one.
* **The flush: `fsync` once a second per room** (the rooms' flushes are spread over the second by a hash of the code), and for all the records when the server is told to stop. **Why not one per turn:** a turn is 50 ms, a disk's flush takes milliseconds, and the server has one thread that every room waits for. **What the flush is for:** the death of the *machine* (a power cut, a kernel panic): data that the process wrote may be in the page cache only. After that death **up to the last second of turns (20) can be missing from the end of the record**, up to ten seconds (200) on a slow disk (the flush is timed, below); a player whose machine is *ahead* of the record (it ran turns that the disk lost) is told `BadRequest` (more turns than were ever sealed) and must start the match from nothing again, which it can: its key is good (S3.87 tests this; the clients of part B do it). The cost is measured below. **The flush is timed** (`Room::sync_record`, the review's L6): a flush that takes more than `RestartConfig::slow_sync_ms` (20 ms of real time, `clock_ms`) doubles that room's interval to its next one, to `max_sync_every_ms` (10 s) at the most, and a quicker one brings it back to `sync_every_ms` (a second), so that a slow or busy disk is asked less often by the rooms that find it slow, and the whole loop waits less; the status says the interval (`record_sync_ms`; S3.123).
* **A torn tail.** A write that the machine's death interrupted leaves a last frame that does not fit, has a wrong checksum, or is a zero fill (the file grew, its data did not reach the disk): the reader drops it, the record is the frames before it, and when the room goes on writing the file is cut to the last good frame first. **Any other fault is corruption**: a bad checksum, length, type or order with a frame behind it, a turn that is not the next one, a checkpoint of a turn that the file does not hold, a head that is wrong in a field: the record is refused. (A length field that is corrupted into one that reaches past the end is read as a torn tail: the reader then stops early, it never hands out altered turns; the fuzz tests pin this.)
* **Bounds.** A record is at most 48 MiB (three times the turn log's limit); all the records of the running rooms together at most 256 MiB (`--restart-budget-mb`: the budget counts the files that rooms hold open, not a stale file whose delete failed, a record that waits on disk or the folder of refused records, which has a bound of its own of the same size); a write that fails (a full disk, a quota, `EFBIG`: S3.89 makes a real one), a record that passes its size, a budget that is used up, and a turn log that passed its limit (the room could no longer give the match to a player who comes back, so a restart could not hold its seats) each **end the record at once**: the file is **deleted** (a record that stops in the middle of a match would bring it back at the wrong tick), the room plays on and its status says why (`record.note`), the log gets a line, and the budget has its bytes back. A match is never harmed by its record. **A delete that fails** (a volume that refuses it) leaves the file of a room that is over, and a restart would bring that room back from it: the store remembers the file as **stale**, the log says so, the status of the room says `record.kept: true` and `record.stale: true` with a note (never `kept: false` while the file is there), and the server tries again **every 10 s** and once more when it is told to stop (S3.109); a line says when the file is gone at last.

### Restoring

At start (`RoomManager::restore_rooms`, called by `main` after the listeners are open) the server reads the `room-*.restart` files of its folder, **newest first** (by the time of their last write: the match that was played a moment ago before one that had been waiting), and **judges** each one (steps 1 and 2; nothing is replayed). What can go on is **queued** (step 3) and replayed **in slices** from the server's loop, which is already running: the server serves while it restores. For each record:

1. **Read and checked all through** (`read_restart_record`; the whole file is read, at most 48 MiB; it is opened once, without following a link and without waiting on a pipe, and the open file is what is checked and read, so nothing can be swapped in between): the magic, every frame's length and checksum, the order of the frames, the turns' numbering, the checkpoints, every field of the head, the commands (the issuer of every one is a seat). A file that fails is **refused**: a line in the log with its name and the reason, the file deleted, nothing else (it is no room: it has no specification that can be trusted). A folder named like a record is not looked at.
2. **Judged.** The record is of this build's rules or it is not: the `protocol` must equal the server's (the game version and the build id may differ: they move with every release, and a deploy that changes no rule must carry the matches over; the log names who wrote the record; the replay below is the defence if a rule changed within a version). The room must be one that this server would make (the same ranges as `POST /rooms`), the record's last write must be less than an hour old (`RestartConfig::max_age_ms`: its players have given up), the server must have room for it, the map must be on this server and **its file must be the one that the match was played on** (the hash in the head), load, and be playable by the roster.
3. **Queued, then replayed in slices.** A record that passed is queued: its code is taken (`POST /rooms` for it answers 409; `GET /rooms/<code>` does not know it until its replay ends). `RoomManager::update` spends at most **5 ms of every pass** of the server on the queue (`RestartConfig::restore_slice_ms`): a room for which a Hello waits first (below), then the newest record. A room's first piece of work reads its record again (the bytes of queued records are not kept) and makes the room: the engine as a client makes it (`init(level, seed, roster)`, the fog, the names), the bots, the keys (`Room::begin_replay`). Then every sealed turn goes into the turn log (what a returning player is given) and into the referee's runner, twenty at a time (`Room::replay_step`; the turns are read one decoded turn at a time by a `RestartTurnReader`), and **at every checkpoint the state hash must be the one that the old server stored for that turn**: a replay that is not the match that was played (a rule that changed within a version, a record that was altered) stops there. The clock is looked at every 20 turns, so a slice ends within 20 turns' work of its time. One room's replay may take **20 s of its own work** (`RestartConfig::replay_budget_ms`: the sum of its slices, not the time that other rooms took between them): beyond it the record is refused as too slow. So is a record that cannot be read again when its turn comes (the file changed while it waited), one whose map changed meanwhile, and one that the turn log cannot hold (its limit, or the server's memory budget). **The crash-loop guard** (`RestartStore::mark_replaying`, the review's L10): before every piece of a record's replay the empty file `<record>.replaying` is made next to it, and it is removed when the piece returns. A start that finds one next to a record (the last run stopped in the middle of that replay: the engine crashed on the match's turns, which were written before they were played, or the process was killed) moves that record to `restart/refused/` unread, with a line in the log and an item in the report, and does not replay it again, so that one record cannot keep a server from ever coming up; the marker of a record that is not there is removed when the folder is prepared. A stop by `SIGTERM` never leaves one (it is looked at between the pieces; S3.124).
4. **Brought back** (`Room::begin_restored`, when **its own** replay ends, **with a clock that starts then**: the time that the replays of the other rooms take is no part of any room's pause, so the 90 s wait of step 5 and the pause cap are not eaten by them; the Hellos that waited for it go through the door at once). The room has its **code** (a Hello with a key finds it as before the restart), the state `running`, the turn count and tick of the record, `restored: {turns, replay_ms, state_hash}` in its status. Every seat of a person is **ABSENT** (`Attendance::seat_restored`): the match is **paused** for everybody until the players come back with their keys (protocol 10: Hello, Welcome, CatchUp or Start + Loaded + Begin + CatchUp, the stream, `CaughtUp`, the referee's comparison at the restored tick, Presence); a seat that the match had **dropped** before the restart (the engine knows: its Drop is in the turns) stays Dropped and its key is told so; a seat without a key is dropped at once. **The bots sit down again** at the restored tick (their tasks are soft, `docs/BOTS.md`: a bot that was running goes on from what it sees; a restored Medium bot has its decisions counted from 0 and looks every 20 ticks again). The record is opened again for appending (a torn tail is cut off): the room goes on writing the same file. **`GET /busy`** (the public answer that a deploy waits on) counts the room as a match that runs, and its held seats as people, for **5 minutes after the restore** (`kRestoredBusyWindowMs`: the players need that long to notice, wait for the server and come back, and a restart now would interrupt them again). After that a room that nobody has come back to counts nothing (it would hold a deploy for the pause cap, 30 minutes to 24 hours), and a player who is back counts as itself (S3.102).
5. **What waits, and for how long.** An absence that a restart causes is **excused**: nobody lost a link. The vote about such a seat opens **after 90 s** (`--restart-vote-seconds`, 30 - 3600; never less than the room's own `hold_vote_seconds`), not a lost link's 30 s: every player has to notice the loss (a closed link at once, 10 s of silence otherwise), wait for the server (a deploy is a build and a start: tens of seconds), reconnect (the clients try every 2 s) and catch up, and a browser tab in the background wakes late; the first player who is back must not be able to vote out the neighbour who is a few seconds behind. The excused absence **adds nothing to the seat's away time** when the seat is back and is **no loss for the flapping rule** (a later loss of the same link is judged as the seat's first: N2.96). Everything else is as for any absent seat: the key holder's budgets (three Hellos a minute, ...) start fresh; **the restart's pause counts toward the match's total pause**, so at the cap (30 minutes by default) every seat that is not back is dropped and **a room whose players never come back ends "everybody left"** by the existing rule (S3.92); the wall-clock limit `run_ms` counts the play before the restart (the 5 s of the dialog and 50 ms a turn) and leaves the pause out (S3.98); the clocks are the new server's own (its uptime starts again: the tests start it at 0.5 s, a second before the wrap of its 32-bit clock, and half way: S3.92).
6. **Not restored: a failed room, with the reason** (decided at once when the record is judged, or when its replay is refused). A record that was read and cannot be brought back (another network protocol, a map that is gone or changed, older than an hour, a replay that does not agree with a stored hash, a room that this server would not make, a replay that would take too long, a turn log that cannot hold the match, no room) becomes a **failed room** with the reason in `reason` (`"ended by a restart of the server: the match was started by v0.1.9 (protocol 11), this server is v0.2.0 (protocol 12), and a match cannot go on across a change of the rules"`): the control interface shows it (state `failed`, the players' names, the ticks), the log and the result file report its end once, a Hello for its code is `NoSuchRoom`, its code is taken until its keep time is over, and its record is **moved to `restart/refused/` and kept for 24 hours** (`RestartConfig::refused_keep_ms`: the folder is purged of older files when the server starts and is held under the same budget as the records, the oldest first; a record that alone passes it is deleted; a record that cannot be read at all is deleted at once): an owner whose map was missing for a moment, or whose server was a version behind, moves the file back (S3.110). **A protocol or rules change therefore ends the old matches, with a clear reason**; a deploy that wants to keep them must not change either number, and should wait for a server with no running match (the deploy job of the CI is meant to do that; it is not part of this section).
7. **A match that had ended** when the server stopped (the record holds the turn that ended it, the room had not finished yet) is **finished at once**, with the referee's final hash (the same as the match that was never interrupted: S3.94), its result rows and the report of its end.

**The server serves while it restores.** The loop runs while the queue is replayed: S3.115 replays a record of an hour of turns and one of the busiest map's four players while another match is made and played, and no pass takes more than its slice and the 10 - 18 ms that the first piece of a room needs (the match that is played meanwhile keeps its 50 ms turns); the `reconnect` part of `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` restores a thousand records for seconds while `/busy` and a new room's Hello are answered at once. What is not sliced: judging the records at the start reads each file whole (about 0.2 s for a record of 38 MiB), and so does the first piece of its room.

**A Hello for a room that is still queued is parked** (`parked_count()`): the connection and the decoded Hello wait in the manager, with no hello timeout, until the room is restored or refused, and then go through the door as any other Hello: a key that fits a seat gets the rejoin `Welcome` and the catch-up, a stranger or a wrong key `MatchRunning`, a refused room `NoSuchRoom`, and a Hello that brings a create block for a public code whose room was refused makes a new room, as ever (S3.116, S3.119). Bounds: `max_pending` (128) Hellos wait in all, the next link is dropped without an answer (a `Reject` is final for a client, a dropped link is tried again), and a room takes as many as its `max_connections`, the next link is dropped without an answer too (it was told `Full`, which is final for a client: silent Hellos of anybody who knows the code could take these places and end a player's way back; the review's L1); a parked connection that closes or sends more than 8 messages after its Hello is dropped, and so is one that has waited `ServerLimits::park_timeout_ms` (a minute: a client takes 10 s without a Welcome for a failure and says Hello again); what a parked peer sends after its Hello reaches the room (S3.118). **`GET /busy`** counts every queued record as a match and its persons as people (a bot is none), so a deploy waits for the restore and a second restart in the middle of one is not let in (S3.120). The control interface does not list a queued record: it is no room yet. The stop flag (`SIGTERM`, `SIGINT`) is looked at between the records that are judged and in every pass of the loop: **every record that is not back (queued or half replayed) stays on disk as it was**, nothing opened again, cut, written or deleted, and the log says how many (S3.106, S3.121; with the real program S3.107).

### Stopping: SIGTERM and SIGKILL

`SIGTERM` and `SIGINT` stop the server within a moment: every room that keeps a record makes it durable (`fsync`; a record that the disk refuses is dropped), **the records stay where they are** (a record is deleted when its room is over, never because the server stops), the rooms that keep none are closed as before; the log says `stopped: N restart record(s) made durable and kept (T ms)` and the exit status is 0. The real program exits **2 ms** after the signal in the test (S3.95); `docker-compose.stack.yml` and `docker-compose.server.yml` set **`stop_grace_period: 15s`** (docker's default is 10 s): the flush is one fsync per running room, a few milliseconds each, so 256 rooms (the most) at a slow volume's 30 ms are 7.7 s; the 15 s are twice that and a margin. **`SIGKILL`** (what docker sends after the grace, a crash, the OOM killer) leaves the records as the turns wrote them: no turn is lost that a player has seen, only the last second of fsync's durability (ten on a slow disk) against the machine's own death (above).

### The limits

| what | number |
|---|---|
| a record | at most 48 MiB (`RestartConfig::max_record_bytes`), a frame at most 1 MiB, a head at most 8 KiB |
| all the records of the running rooms | 256 MiB (`--restart-budget-mb`, 1 - 4096); files that are not open (stale, queued, refused) are not counted |
| the file's durability | `write()` for every turn (survives the process), `fsync` once a second per room and at SIGTERM (survives the machine up to the last second; ten on a slow disk) |
| a record's age | restored when its last write is less than an hour old |
| the replay | at most 20 s of a room's own work in all (`replay_budget_ms`, the sum of its slices); 5 ms of every pass of the server for the whole queue (`restore_slice_ms`); the turn log's own limits and the server's memory budget apply |
| a Hello that waits for its room | at most a minute (`park_timeout_ms`); 128 in all (`max_pending`: the next link is dropped), a room's `max_connections` (32) for one room (the next is told `Full`); 8 messages after the Hello |
| the vote after a restart | 90 s (`--restart-vote-seconds`), never less than the room's `hold_vote_seconds` |
| the cap after a restart | the room's `max_pause_seconds` (30 minutes by default), the restart's pause counts |
| rooms | the server's `--max-rooms`; one record and one open file a room (a room that keeps a record holds its file open for the whole match); a waiting room has none |
| keys | the record's: the same 16 bytes per seat as in the Welcome |

### Options and the stack

`--restart-dir DIR` (default: the folder `restart` of `--results-dir`; without a results folder the server keeps none and says so; an explicit folder that cannot be used (it is not a folder, it is locked by another server, or a probe file cannot be written in it: a read-only volume, a quota, a full disk) stops the server with status 1, the default one is given up with a line in the log), `--no-restart-records` (the two exclude each other: status 2), `--restart-vote-seconds N`, `--restart-budget-mb N`. Only rooms that hold seats keep a record (every room does unless `--no-reconnect` or the room's `"reconnect": false`). **One server to a folder**: the store locks the folder for its life (the file `.lock`, `flock`; Windows: opened with no sharing), because two servers on one folder would write each other's records. A second server cannot take it: with an explicit `--restart-dir` it stops with status 1, with the default folder it logs that restart records are off and runs without them. The lock goes with the process, however it ends; the temporary files of a record that was being made are removed only by the server that holds the lock (S3.104, S3.105). The status JSON of a room has `"record": {"kept", "stale", "bytes", "note"}` and `"restored": {"turns", "replay_ms", "state_hash"} | null`.

### Tests

`test_lockstep` **N2.96** (the attendance of a restart: every seat absent and excused, dropped seats stay dropped, the vote after 90 s and not 30, nobody can win it while nobody is there, an excused absence adds nothing and is no loss for the flapping rule, the cap, the countdown, from four clocks), **N2.97** (a host restored from the turns of its predecessor: the same state hash at the restored tick and at every later tick when the same turns follow, the same log bytes, the three machines come back through the door and the match goes on identical), **N2.98** (the hooks: once for every turn, in order, after the log and **before any client has been sent the turn**; the checkpoints), **N2.99** (what a restored host refuses; a seat without a key; a match restored with no turn at all, which needs no command of the pre-start); `test_server` **S3.82** (the record's format, mode, name, atomic creation, torn tail, budget), **S3.83** (the reader under every prefix and every flipped bit of a record, garbage, hostile lengths, every kind of wrong frame and head: nothing crashes, nothing altered is accepted), **S3.84** (a match through a restart with SIGTERM's semantics: the keys, the checkpoints equal the machines' own hashes, an independent replay of the record has the restored hash, the machines come back, the match ends identical), **S3.85** (every refusal, with its reason in the status, the JSON and the log, and the record deleted), **S3.86** (files that are no records), **S3.87** (a torn tail; a record that lacks the last seconds and the machines that are ahead), **S3.88** (the record holds every turn that any machine has been sent, at every 10 ms step), **S3.89** (the disk's limits, a real `EFBIG`), **S3.90** (the life of a record), **S3.91** (no key in any text), **S3.92** (the vote after a restart from three clocks, a seat dropped before the restart, the cap), **S3.93** (the bots), **S3.94** (a match that had ended), **S3.97** (the switch), **S3.98** (the room's limit), **S3.99** (no client address in a record), **S3.100** (the measurements; `ANTS_PERSIST_MEASURE_LONG=1` adds the longest plays), **S3.102** (a restored room counts as a match that runs in `GET /busy`, its held seats as people, for 5 minutes from the restore to the millisecond and then nothing; a player who is back counts as itself, in a live room a seat whose link is lost is no person who is there; a room that could not be restored counts nothing), and the real program: **S3.95** (SIGTERM) and **S3.96** (SIGKILL), with machines over real TCP sockets that find the room by themselves, **S3.101** (opt-in: the same match against a real **docker container** that the operator made from the server's image with a volume at `/results`: `ANTS_PERSIST_CONTAINER` names it, `ANTS_PERSIST_GAME_PORT`, `ANTS_PERSIST_CTL_PORT` and `ANTS_PERSIST_SECRET` say how to reach it; it runs `docker stop` (exit code 0 within the grace), `docker start`, the machines find the room by themselves, the match ends in one state, the record on the volume is mode 600 in a folder of mode 700 and is gone at the end, no key is in `docker logs`; it was run against the image with and without the stack's `read_only` / `cap_drop: ALL` / `no-new-privileges`), and `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` (its `options` part: the options of the records and their edges; its `reconnect` part: the record's mode, SIGTERM and SIGKILL with the real games, a thousand records restored while the server answers). Each test was shown to fail without the code it tests (`docs/audit/persist_notes.md` lists the 71 faults: 70 are caught, one is an equivalent mutant whose line the product keeps as it was). The server, net and lock-step suites also build with GCC 12 and `-Werror` on Linux and pass there (the real-process tests included), and run clean under AddressSanitizer + UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer.

**The review's tests** (the findings of the independent review, `docs/audit/persist_notes.md`): `test_server` **S3.103** (M1: a record may hold 1,800,000 turns and not one more, judged from a frame's header; a record read as Streaming keeps no turns and gives them again one at a time; the 47 MiB hostile file is refused at the start; keep time and run limit at most 24 h), **S3.104** and **S3.105** (M5: the folder's lock between two stores, across a killed process, and with the real program), **S3.106** (M2: the budgets, a slice a pass and a cap a room; seven records queued and restored newest first, their codes taken, `/busy`; the cap; the stop flag while the records are judged), **S3.107** (M2: the real program told to stop in the middle of a restore), `test_lobby` **N4.21** and `test_server` **S3.108** (L3: the record is made before the Start is sent: the lobby's hook, and the folder looked at when each Start leaves the server), **S3.109** (L1: a delete that fails, here by a folder that takes the file's name: the stale file, the status and its JSON, the cadence of the retries, the stop's retry), **S3.110** (L2: a refused record is kept for a day in `restart/refused/`, whole and private, and restored when the owner moves it back; a corrupt one is deleted; the purge at start; the folder's budget), **S3.111** (L5: the probe: a folder where a write fails is refused with the reason and holds no lock), **S3.112** (L8: a record is opened once, and a link, a folder and a pipe named like a record are refused as not regular files, the pipe without waiting on it; the size limit is judged on the open file, to the byte).

**The review of phase 2** (the independent review of the clients' way back and of reconnect being on for every room; its findings, the fixes, the tests, the mutants and the browser runs are in `docs/audit/persist_notes.md`; no network protocol change, it stays 12, no change of the simulation). What it changed is said where it belongs: a demo room's cap and the eviction of an abandoned one (M1) and a Hello with a key that makes no room (M3) in "The server around it", the key's moment, its three hours and the seat in the address (M3, L2) in "The keys", the parked bound (L1) in "Restoring", the timed flush (L6) in "Writing", the crash-loop guard (L10) in "Restoring" step 3, the quit in the lobby phases (L3) in "Leaving", and L8 and L9 in the known limits. A re-check of these fixes found five small items from the fixes themselves, fixed the same way (the notes say which: N1 - N5). Three more are said here. **M2** the page tells the game when a player leaves on purpose: the header's Menu button, the footer's Menu link and the picture selector's yes call the export `ants_leave_match()` (`Application::leave_network_match`, `NetGame::leave()`: Leave is sent and the key is let go of) after the player confirmed and before the page moves, so the room drops the seat at once instead of holding it for the pause cap; a closed tab and a reload still hold it (RA6.3, `web_aspect_key_check.js`, the browser check's part `leave`). **L4** `web/shell.html` cancels F2 and F3, the vote's keys, like F1 (`ANTS_PAGE.cancelsBrowserKey`; F5 and F12 stay the player's). **L5** the vote about a machine's own seat is not shown to it and cannot be cast by it (`NetGame::pause_info`, `vote`; RJ1.23). **L7** a desktop `--join NAME:PORT` looks the name up once, at the join (`resolve_host`: the first IPv4 address, else the first IPv6 one with its scope, as the start menu's lookup; `JoinTarget::resolved`), and every link of the way back goes to that address (RJ1.24, N3.9). The seat in the address (L2): the game tells the page the seat that the room gave it (`Application::set_on_seat_known`: in the waiting room, once, and again if it changes; the web build calls `window.antsSeatKnown(seat)`) and the page writes `seat=N` into the address with `history.replaceState`, as it takes `name` out (`ANTS_PAGE.withSeat`; never for a frame or a game on this computer), so that a reload takes this window's own seat and key even when two windows of one browser play one room (RA10.1, `web_name_check.js`, the browser check's part `reload`).

**The tests of the restore in slices** (`test_server`): **S3.113** (`RestartTurnReader`: exactly the turns of a record of every kind, a torn tail, every kind of changed bytes), **S3.114** (`Room::begin_replay` and `replay_step`: slices of 1, 7 and 100 ms give the same room as one call, a slice ends within 20 turns' work of its time, the cap is on the sum of a room's slices), **S3.115** (a long record is replayed while a match is made and played: the passes, the longest pass, `/busy`; `ANTS_RESTORE_MEASURE_LONG=1` adds the longest real match), **S3.116** (a parked Hello gets the rejoin `Welcome` and a catch-up that ends at the restored state; strangers and wrong keys are told `MatchRunning`), **S3.117** (the order), **S3.118** (the bounds of parking, a closed or flooding connection, the timeout, what a parked peer sends), **S3.119** (a record refused in its replay, changed or swapped while it waited, or whose map changed), **S3.120** (`/busy` at every pass), **S3.121** (a stop leaves every record byte for byte), **S3.122** (a room's clocks start when its own replay ends); S3.106 (rewritten: the queue, the slice, the cap) and S3.107 (the real program told to stop) are the older tests of the restore. Each was shown to fail without the code it tests (`docs/audit/persist_notes.md`).

### Measured (S3.100; macOS, Apple silicon, Release build, on a machine that shared its cores with other work: the load average was about 40 on 10 cores, so these are upper bounds, not a quiet machine's best)

* **A record's size**: a minute of play adds **21.7 KB** when nobody gives an order (1,200 turns of 17 bytes and 60 checkpoints), **25.9 KB** for three players who each give an order every 0.7 s and **27.4 KB** for four (the machines' orders are random group moves: busy play). A match of 11 minutes 40 s on TREASURE with four players (13,999 turns, the test's longest): **350 KiB**; a 12-minute match is about 14,400 turns and 360 KiB.
* **The cost of writing**: **2.1 - 2.6 microseconds a turn** (100,000 turns in 0.21 - 0.26 s: one `write()` of about 25 bytes); a flush of a second's turns costs **0.04 ms on average and 0.5 - 0.6 ms at the worst** (macOS's `fsync` does not flush the drive's own cache). The same test on Linux (Debian 12, GCC 12, inside a docker VM, real `fsync` on its overlay file system): **0.56 microseconds a turn, 1.35 ms on average and 6.6 ms at the worst for a flush**. The room does one a second, spread over the second by a hash of the code, and they all run on the server's one thread, so 256 rooms cost 256 times the average: macOS 0.04 ms x 256 is about **10 ms of every second**; the Linux VM 1.35 ms x 256 is about **350 ms, a third of the thread**; a volume at 30 ms a flush x 256 would be **more than a second a second** at one flush a second, so the rooms that find it slow ask less often (their intervals double up to 10 s, L6): about **0.77 s of every second**, and a power cut can take up to ten seconds of turns. A flusher thread (the `write()` stays on the thread, only the `fsync` moves) is phase 2 material.
* **Bringing a match back**: TINY with three players, 5 minutes of play (5,599 turns): **0.10 s**; **TREASURE with four players, 11 minutes 40 s of play (13,999 turns: the engine's busiest map and the longest match): 2.3 - 2.9 s** (4,800 - 6,000 turns a second; a full 12-minute match is 14,400 turns, so about 2.5 - 3 s). The server serves while it restores (the replay runs in slices of 5 ms a pass: S3.115; the longest pass of the 13,599-turn match took 17.5 ms). Busy play replays at about 4,200 turns a second here, so the cap of 20 s of work is about 80,000 turns of four players' play (extrapolated): a longer match is refused as too slow. (The same replay under AddressSanitizer + UBSan on an unoptimised build takes minutes: the suites' bounds are for the Release build and the sanitizer runs print the numbers without the bounds' failing.)
* **Stopping**: the real program exits **2 ms** after SIGTERM on macOS and 4 ms on Linux (S3.95), `docker stop` of the container took **0.18 - 0.23 s** (exit code 0; docker's own round trip is most of it), `docker stop` of the stack as it was then (no `--reconnect`, which was the default: nothing to keep) 0.19 s.

### Limits (documented, not fixed)

* **A waiting room is not kept** (it has no match to bring back): a restart empties it, its players join again (a public room is made again by the first Hello that brings a create block; a room of the control interface is gone and its owner makes it again).
* **The machine's death** can take the last second of turns, ten seconds on a slow disk (see "Writing"); the players ahead of the record start the match from nothing again.
* **A vote that was won and whose Drop was not sealed yet is undone by a restart**: the votes are not in the record and the seat's Drop is a turn, which is in the record only once it was sealed; the vote starts again after the restart (and the seat is still held).
* **A pause longer than an hour does not survive a restart**: a record's age counts from its last write, and a pause writes nothing (no turn is sealed), so a room that waited for a player for more than an hour (`--max-pause-seconds` allows 24 hours) is refused as given up at the next restart, though its pause has not reached its cap.
* **The attendance's own bookkeeping is not in the record**: how long each seat was away and how much pause the match had spent before the restart start again at 0 (a match that had paused for 20 of its 30 minutes has 30 again); the cap still bounds the wait for any one restart.
* **The restore takes the server's time in slices, but not all of it is sliced**: judging the records at the start reads each file whole (about 5 ms a MiB: a record at the limit of 48 MiB takes a quarter of a second, and the first piece of its room reads it again), and until a room is back its players' Hellos wait (a minute at the most, then they say Hello again) and `GET /rooms` does not list it. A restart in the middle of a restore leaves the records as they were and the next start does it all again.
* **Windows** sets no permissions on the folder and the record (they take those of the folder: it must be private), and its flush is `FlushFileBuffers`.
* **A record is as private as its folder**: whoever can read the volume can take the seats of the matches that run (they hold the keys). The folder is mode 700 on POSIX.
* **The application of part B** must show "the server is restarting" while `NetGame` retries and skip the start dialog on a rejoin (`NetGame` itself starts from nothing on a `BadRequest` to a Hello that carries turns, and flags the events).

## State hash (`SimulationEngine::state_hash`)

FNV-1a 64 over the gameplay state in seven parts (engine, players, grid, food, ants, paths, droppers). Everything that can influence a later tick is in it (both PRNG states, clocks, every ant field, the
occupancy grid, every map cell, hills, food objects, statistics, eggs, alliances, invitations, queued path requests, flower droppers); what only presents the simulation (audio and news queues, effects, the
per-viewer fog, names) is not. `tests/test_sim/test_commands.cpp` changes every listed field in turn and demands a different hash, runs two engines with permuted command arrival for 600 ticks and three
seeds and demands the same hash every tick, and demands that an engine that played another map equals a fresh engine (`init()` used to leave stale cell flags behind).

## Milestones

1. **Command layer, issuer and ownership checks, `Stop` as a command, `init()` reset, state hash, removal of the alliance auto-accept, two-engine loopback test: shipped (v0.0.43).**
2. **Lock-step core: protocol, sequencer, runner, host and client sessions, in-memory network: shipped (v0.0.44).** (The plan's order changed: the turn logic needs no third-party code, so it was built and tested
   first, over a simulated network with latency and jitter.)
3. **Framed TCP for LAN and development, the room handshake and the start barrier: shipped (v0.0.45).**
4. **The application: host / join, the room with names and thumbs, the runner instead of the wall-clock tick driver, predicted click feedback, waiting and desync messages, roster and drop-out through the
   turn stream, names on the command line: shipped (v0.0.46).** Still open from the original plan: native versus WebAssembly golden hashes.
4b. **Host migration** (the match continues when the host leaves, as in the original): peer links between the guests (a TCP address directory for LAN), the turn log, election with epochs, resync, the sequencer
   role moving to a surviving machine, the events and messages in the game, tests with the host dying at many points, double failures and a partitioned old host: **shipped (v0.0.47).**
5. *(dropped on 2026-09-30)* WebRTC data channels with ICE / STUN / TURN (native via libdatachannel, browser via RTCPeerConnection) and the signaling service: internet play is provided from outside the game (a launcher / lobby with its own
   NAT traversal); the game keeps the `Connection` seam; the browser build got its transport in v0.0.84 (a WebSocket to the game server, below), not WebRTC.
6. *(dropped with 5)* Docker / nginx / TURN deployment files.
7. **Alliance dialogs (the ally attack confirmation, string 4; the invitation questions, strings 1 - 3): shipped (v0.0.50).** CHECKGO elimination rules (the match also ends when nobody is alive or one side leads alone): **shipped (v0.0.62).**
8. **LAN discovery (UDP announcements of open rooms, the browser, `--lan-list`): shipped (v0.0.78).** The in-game host / join screens are the desktop start menu's (v0.0.97, rooms of the game server); a room on the local network has none. The launcher interface is the command line (`--host`, `--join`, `--room`, `--start-when`) and the server's control interface; replays are not built.
9. **Reconnect** (a player whose connection is lost is held, the others vote, the player comes back from the server's turn log): **release A (protocol 10: the building blocks, the sessions and the server, behind a switch, off by default) shipped in v0.0.98** (see "Reconnect" above); release B (the game's clients) **shipped in v0.6.0** and turned it on: `NetGame` comes back by itself, and the application's overlay, vote, catch-up screen, key storage and "Rejoin" are built ("What the clients do in release B"); the switch is on, the web page's button and the browser check are built ("The switch, the front page's Rejoin button and the check in a real browser"), and the review is done.

10. **The match clock waits for the "Get ready to play!" dialog** (network protocol 12: the host seals the first turn 5 s after the match began, a machine closes its dialog when its first turn executes; a deliberate deviation from the original, see "Protocol 12"): **shipped (v0.2.0).**

Open for milestone 3 and later: a float-determinism audit across native and WebAssembly (the flower dropper's type draw uses doubles), native versus wasm golden hashes.
